## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the SensorFrame model

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_frame.dart`:

The raw EventChannel payload is a `Map<dynamic, dynamic>` with these keys (confirmed
from native side — do NOT assume additional keys exist):
{

"timestamp_us": int,        // microseconds since epoch

"accel_x": double,

"accel_y": double,

"accel_z": double,          // accelerometer in m/s²

"gyro_x": double,

"gyro_y": double,

"gyro_z": double,           // gyroscope in rad/s

"rotation_matrix": List<double>,  // 9-element row-major 3×3 matrix, nullable

}

Define `SensorFrame` as an immutable class (use `@immutable` annotation):

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

@immutable
class SensorFrame {
  final int timestampUs;
  final double accelX;
  final double accelY;
  final double accelZ;
  final double gyroX;
  final double gyroY;
  final double gyroZ;
  final List<double>? rotationMatrix; // null if native did not emit it

  const SensorFrame({
    required this.timestampUs,
    required this.accelX,
    required this.accelY,
    required this.accelZ,
    required this.gyroX,
    required this.gyroY,
    required this.gyroZ,
    this.rotationMatrix,
  });
}
```

Do NOT add `toJson` / `fromJson` in this task — serialization is out of scope.

### Step 2: Define SensorParseException

In the same file `sensor_frame.dart`, below the class definition:

```dart
class SensorParseException implements Exception {
  final String message;
  final Map<dynamic, dynamic>? rawPayload;

  const SensorParseException(this.message, {this.rawPayload});

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'SensorParseException: $message | raw: $rawPayload';
}
```

### Step 3: Implement SensorStreamParser

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';
import 'sensor_frame.dart';

class SensorStreamParser {
  static const _channelName = 'com.mayasabhaxr.sensor/stream';

  final EventChannel _channel;

  SensorStreamParser({EventChannel? channel})
      : _channel = channel ??
            const EventChannel(_channelName);

  Stream<SensorFrame> get frames => _channel
      .receiveBroadcastStream()
      .map(_parseEvent)
      .handleError(_onChannelError);

  SensorFrame _parseEvent(dynamic event) {
    if (event is! Map) {
      throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected Map, got ${event.runtimeType}',
        rawPayload: null,
      );
    }
    final raw = Map<dynamic, dynamic>.from(event as Map);
    return _toFrame(raw);
  }

  SensorFrame _toFrame(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw) {
    try {
      final rotationRaw = raw['rotation_matrix'];
      List<double>? rotationMatrix;
      if (rotationRaw != null) {
        final list = List<dynamic>.from(rotationRaw as List);
        if (list.length != 9) {
          throw SensorParseException(
            'rotation_matrix must have 9 elements, got ${list.length}',
            rawPayload: raw,
          );
        }
        rotationMatrix = list.map((e) => (e as num).toDouble()).toList();
      }

      return SensorFrame(
        timestampUs: _requireInt(raw, 'timestamp_us'),
        accelX: _requireDouble(raw, 'accel_x'),
        accelY: _requireDouble(raw, 'accel_y'),
        accelZ: _requireDouble(raw, 'accel_z'),
        gyroX: _requireDouble(raw, 'gyro_x'),
        gyroY: _requireDouble(raw, 'gyro_y'),
        gyroZ: _requireDouble(raw, 'gyro_z'),
        rotationMatrix: rotationMatrix,
      );
    } on SensorParseException {
      rethrow;
    } catch (e) {
      throw SensorParseException(
        'Unexpected parse error: $e',
        rawPayload: raw,
      );
    }
  }

  int _requireInt(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw, String key) {
    final val = raw[key];
    if (val == null) throw SensorParseException('Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (val is int) return val;
    if (val is num) return val.toInt();
    throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected int for $key, got ${val.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  double _requireDouble(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw, String key) {
    final val = raw[key];
    if (val == null) throw SensorParseException('Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (val is double) return val;
    if (val is num) return val.toDouble();
    throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected double for $key, got ${val.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  void _onChannelError(Object error, StackTrace stack) {
    // Errors from the native EventChannel (e.g. PlatformException) propagate
    // downstream to stream subscribers — do NOT swallow them here.
    // Callers decide whether to log, retry, or surface to UI.
  }
}
```

The `EventChannel` constructor is injected so unit tests can supply a mock channel
without touching the platform layer.

### Step 4: Write unit tests

Create `test/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser_test.dart`.

Use `StreamController<dynamic>` to simulate the EventChannel broadcast stream.
Mock `EventChannel.receiveBroadcastStream()` by injecting a fake channel whose
`receiveBroadcastStream` returns the controller's stream.

Test cases to cover — implement ALL of them:

1. **Valid full payload** → `SensorFrame` fields match input values exactly
2. **Valid payload with null rotation_matrix** → `SensorFrame.rotationMatrix` is null
3. **Missing required key (`accel_x` absent)** → `SensorParseException` is thrown with
   message containing `"Missing key: accel_x"`
4. **Wrong type for `timestamp_us` (String instead of int)** → `SensorParseException`
   thrown, not a `TypeError`
5. **`rotation_matrix` with 7 elements instead of 9** → `SensorParseException` thrown
   with message containing `"9 elements"`
6. **Non-Map event (e.g. raw String arrives)** → `SensorParseException` thrown, stream
   does not crash — subsequent valid events are still parsed correctly
7. **Multiple sequential valid events** → stream emits all frames in order, no frames
   dropped

---

## API / Data Contract

Native → Dart EventChannel payload (raw `Map<dynamic, dynamic>`):
{

"timestamp_us": int,          // required — microseconds since Unix epoch

"accel_x": double | int,      // required — m/s², coerced to double if int

"accel_y": double | int,      // required

"accel_z": double | int,      // required

"gyro_x": double | int,       // required — rad/s

"gyro_y": double | int,       // required

"gyro_z": double | int,       // required

"rotation_matrix": List<num>, // optional — null if sensor unavailable;

//   if present, must have exactly 9 elements

}

Any key not in this list must be silently ignored — do not throw for unknown keys.
This allows the native side to add future fields without breaking the parser.

- Do NOT modify any native platform files (Kotlin, Swift, ObjC) — this task is Dart-only
- Do NOT create a Riverpod provider in this task — `SensorStreamParser` is a plain Dart
  class; provider wiring is a separate task
- Do NOT modify `captured_frame.dart` or `capture_controller.dart`
- Do NOT add logging via `print()` — if debug tracing is needed, use `dart:developer`'s
  `log()` behind a `kDebugMode` guard, but prefer leaving it out entirely in this task
- Do NOT add any UI code, widgets, or imports from `package:flutter/material.dart`
  in the parser or model files — these are pure Dart; only `package:flutter/foundation.dart`
  and `package:flutter/services.dart` are permitted

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Native emits an `int` for a field declared as `double` (common on Android) →
      coerce via `.toDouble()`, never throw; `_requireDouble` already handles this —
      verify the implementation covers `num` supertype, not just `double`
- [ ] Native emits `null` for the entire event (channel teardown signal on some Android
      versions) → treat as a `SensorParseException`, do not emit a frame, do not crash
      the stream
- [ ] `rotation_matrix` is present but empty list (`[]`) → throw `SensorParseException`
      with message `"rotation_matrix must have 9 elements, got 0"`
- [ ] EventChannel emits a `PlatformException` (e.g. sensor hardware unavailable) →
      let it propagate as a stream error; do NOT catch and swallow in `_onChannelError`
- [ ] Very high-frequency stream (>200 Hz on flagship devices) → parser must not
      allocate objects unnecessarily; avoid `Map.from()` inside hot path if profiling
      shows GC pressure (acceptable to defer this optimization — note it with a
      `// PERF:` comment)

  # NEW FEATURE: SensorFrame Value Type — yaw, pitch, roll, gyroMag, accelMag, timestamp
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Define the canonical `SensorFrame` immutable value type for the sensor pipeline.
This model is the shared data contract between `SensorStreamParser` (which produces it)
and all downstream consumers (AR pose estimator, Riverpod providers, capture pipeline).
Getting the field types, names, and validation rules exactly right here prevents
breakage in every layer that depends on it.

- [ ] Define `SensorFrame` with exactly six fields: `yaw`, `pitch`, `roll`,
      `gyroMag`, `accelMag`, `timestamp`
- [ ] Define field units, value ranges, and nullability contracts as inline doc comments
- [ ] Implement `SensorFrame.fromRaw(Map<dynamic, dynamic>)` factory that derives
      `yaw/pitch/roll` from raw accelerometer + gyroscope components and computes
      `gyroMag` and `accelMag` as vector magnitudes
- [ ] Implement `SensorFrame.lerp(SensorFrame a, SensorFrame b, double t)` static
      method for downstream interpolation use
- [ ] Implement `==`, `hashCode`, and `toString()` overrides
- [ ] Write unit tests covering factory construction, magnitude computation,
      angle derivation, lerp, equality, and out-of-range field values

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_frame.dart` — THIS FILE WILL BE REPLACED.
   Read the current stub definition (fields, `SensorParseException`) before rewriting;
   preserve `SensorParseException` exactly as-is in the same file.
2. `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser.dart` — read how it currently
   calls `SensorFrame(...)` so the new constructor signature does not break it;
   you will need to update the parser's `_toFrame()` call site after rewriting the model.
3. `test/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser_test.dart` — read existing test
   fixtures (raw Map shapes) so you reuse the same payload structure in new tests.
4. `pubspec.yaml` — confirm `vector_math` is listed as a dependency; add
   `vector_math: ^2.1.4` if absent (needed for `radians()` / trig helpers).

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Rewrite SensorFrame in sensor_frame.dart

Replace the existing `SensorFrame` class definition (leave `SensorParseException`
untouched below it). The new definition:

```dart
import 'dart:math' as math;
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// Canonical sensor snapshot produced once per native EventChannel event.
///
/// All angles are in **radians**. Magnitude fields are in SI units.
/// [timestamp] is microseconds since Unix epoch (matches native `timestamp_us`).
@immutable
class SensorFrame {
  /// Rotation around the vertical (Z) axis. Range: [-π, π]. Radians.
  final double yaw;

  /// Rotation around the lateral (X) axis. Range: [-π/2, π/2]. Radians.
  final double pitch;

  /// Rotation around the longitudinal (Y) axis. Range: [-π, π]. Radians.
  final double roll;

  /// Euclidean magnitude of the gyroscope vector (rad/s). Always ≥ 0.
  final double gyroMag;

  /// Euclidean magnitude of the accelerometer vector (m/s²). Always ≥ 0.
  final double accelMag;

  /// Microseconds since Unix epoch. Must be > 0.
  final int timestamp;

  const SensorFrame({
    required this.yaw,
    required this.pitch,
    required this.roll,
    required this.gyroMag,
    required this.accelMag,
    required this.timestamp,
  });
```

### Step 2: Implement SensorFrame.fromRaw() factory

Add the factory constructor directly below the primary constructor.
It receives the same raw `Map<dynamic, dynamic>` that `SensorStreamParser._toFrame()`
currently works with.

Derivation rules — implement exactly as specified:

**accelMag:**
accelMag = sqrt(accel_x² + accel_y² + accel_z²)

**gyroMag:**
gyroMag = sqrt(gyro_x² + gyro_y² + gyro_z²)

**pitch** (tilt forward/back from gravity vector):
pitch = atan2(-accel_x, sqrt(accel_y² + accel_z²))

**roll** (tilt left/right from gravity vector):
roll = atan2(accel_y, accel_z)

**yaw** (heading from gyroscope integration — single-frame approximation):
yaw = atan2(gyro_y, gyro_x)
Note: single-frame yaw from gyro is a coarse estimate; a Kalman filter layer above
this model is responsible for drift correction. Do not add filtering here.

```dart
factory SensorFrame.fromRaw(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw) {
  double _d(String key) {
    final v = raw[key];
    if (v == null) throw SensorParseException('Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (v is double) return v;
    if (v is num) return v.toDouble();
    throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected num for $key, got ${v.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  int _i(String key) {
    final v = raw[key];
    if (v == null) throw SensorParseException('Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (v is int) return v;
    if (v is num) return v.toInt();
    throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected int for $key, got ${v.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  final ax = _d('accel_x'), ay = _d('accel_y'), az = _d('accel_z');
  final gx = _d('gyro_x'), gy = _d('gyro_y'), gz = _d('gyro_z');

  final accelMag = math.sqrt(ax * ax + ay * ay + az * az);
  final gyroMag  = math.sqrt(gx * gx + gy * gy + gz * gz);
  final pitch    = math.atan2(-ax, math.sqrt(ay * ay + az * az));
  final roll     = math.atan2(ay, az);
  final yaw      = math.atan2(gy, gx);

  return SensorFrame(
    yaw: yaw,
    pitch: pitch,
    roll: roll,
    gyroMag: gyroMag,
    accelMag: accelMag,
    timestamp: _i('timestamp_us'),
  );
}
```

### Step 3: Implement SensorFrame.lerp()

Add as a static method. `t` is clamped to [0.0, 1.0] — do not trust the caller to
clamp it:

```dart
static SensorFrame lerp(SensorFrame a, SensorFrame b, double t) {
  final tc = t.clamp(0.0, 1.0);
  return SensorFrame(
    yaw:       a.yaw      + (b.yaw      - a.yaw)      * tc,
    pitch:     a.pitch    + (b.pitch    - a.pitch)    * tc,
    roll:      a.roll     + (b.roll     - a.roll)     * tc,
    gyroMag:   a.gyroMag  + (b.gyroMag  - a.gyroMag)  * tc,
    accelMag:  a.accelMag + (b.accelMag - a.accelMag) * tc,
    timestamp: a.timestamp + ((b.timestamp - a.timestamp) * tc).round(),
  );
}
```

### Step 4: Implement ==, hashCode, toString

```dart
@override
bool operator ==(Object other) =>
    identical(this, other) ||
    other is SensorFrame &&
        yaw == other.yaw &&
        pitch == other.pitch &&
        roll == other.roll &&
        gyroMag == other.gyroMag &&
        accelMag == other.accelMag &&
        timestamp == other.timestamp;

@override
int get hashCode => Object.hash(yaw, pitch, roll, gyroMag, accelMag, timestamp);

@override
String toString() =>
    'SensorFrame(yaw: ${yaw.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
    'pitch: ${pitch.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
    'roll: ${roll.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
    'gyroMag: ${gyroMag.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
    'accelMag: ${accelMag.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
    'timestamp: $timestamp)';
```

### Step 5: Update SensorStreamParser call site

In `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser.dart`, replace the body of
`_toFrame(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw)` with a single delegation:

```dart
SensorFrame _toFrame(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw) {
  return SensorFrame.fromRaw(raw);
}
```

Remove all field extraction logic that previously lived in `_toFrame()` — it is now
owned by `SensorFrame.fromRaw()`. Keep `_requireInt` and `_requireDouble` helpers
only if they are used elsewhere in the parser; delete them if not.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] All three accelerometer components are zero (device in free fall or bad sensor
      read) → `accelMag = 0.0`, `pitch` and `roll` both become `atan2(0, 0) = 0.0`;
      this is mathematically valid — do not throw, do not special-case
- [ ] All three gyroscope components are zero (device stationary) →
      `gyroMag = 0.0`, `yaw = atan2(0, 0) = 0.0`; valid — do not throw
- [ ] `timestamp_us` is 0 or negative → do NOT throw in `fromRaw()`; callers upstream
      in the parser are responsible for timestamp validation; `SensorFrame` is a dumb
      value type
- [ ] `lerp` called with `t = double.nan` → `clamp(0.0, 1.0)` on NaN returns the
      lower bound `0.0` in Dart; this is acceptable — add a `// NOTE:` comment
      documenting this behaviour but do not add a NaN guard
- [ ] Very large gyroscope values (e.g. device dropped, spike > 100 rad/s) → parse
      and store as-is; range clamping is the responsibility of the AR pose layer above

---

## Constraints

- `yaw`, `pitch`, `roll` must be in **radians** — never degrees; the consuming AR layer
  expects radians and will not convert
- `gyroMag` and `accelMag` must always be ≥ 0 — the `sqrt()` guarantee covers this;
  do not add an explicit `abs()` call (it is redundant and signals confusion)
- `SensorFrame` must remain `const`-constructible via the primary constructor — the
  `fromRaw` factory is not `const` (it computes values at runtime), which is expected
- Do not use `vector_math` inside `SensorFrame` itself — use `dart:math` only; the
  `pubspec.yaml` check in "Files to Inspect" is for potential future use by the pose
  estimator, not for this file
- `lerp` timestamp interpolation must use `.round()` not `.toInt()` (truncation
  introduces consistent negative bias over many frames)

# NEW FEATURE: Stability Score Computation from SensorFrame
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

## Task Description

Implement a pure-Dart `StabilityScorer` that consumes a sliding window of `SensorFrame`
values and produces a normalised `StabilityScore` in the range [0.0, 1.0], where
`1.0` means the device is perfectly still and `0.0` means maximum detected motion.
This is a stateful, side-effect-free computation class — no UI, no Riverpod, no I/O.

- [ ] Define the `StabilityScore` immutable value type with `score`, `confidence`,
      and `timestamp` fields
- [ ] Implement `StabilityScorer` with a configurable sliding window over `SensorFrame`
      history, computing score from `gyroMag` variance, `accelMag` variance, and
      angular velocity (yaw/pitch/roll delta between consecutive frames)
- [ ] Implement `StabilityScorer.add(SensorFrame)` → `StabilityScore` — the single
      public mutation + computation method
- [ ] Implement `StabilityScorer.reset()` — clears window state
- [ ] Write unit tests covering score extremes, window eviction, confidence ramp-up,
      and monotonicity under increasing motion

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define StabilityScore

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_score.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// Normalised device-stillness score for a moment in time.
///
/// [score] ∈ [0.0, 1.0]. 1.0 = perfectly still. 0.0 = maximum motion.
/// [confidence] ∈ [0.0, 1.0]. Rises linearly as the sliding window fills.
///   Below 1.0 the score is computed from fewer frames than the full window
///   and should be treated as provisional by callers.
/// [timestamp] matches the [SensorFrame.timestamp] of the most recent frame
///   added to the scorer — microseconds since Unix epoch.
@immutable
class StabilityScore {
  final double score;
  final double confidence;
  final int timestamp;

  const StabilityScore({
    required this.score,
    required this.confidence,
    required this.timestamp,
  });

  /// Sentinel returned before any frame has been added.
  static const empty = StabilityScore(
    score: 0.0,
    confidence: 0.0,
    timestamp: 0,
  );

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      identical(this, other) ||
      other is StabilityScore &&
          score == other.score &&
          confidence == other.confidence &&
          timestamp == other.timestamp;

  @override
  int get hashCode => Object.hash(score, confidence, timestamp);

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'StabilityScore(score: ${score.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
      'confidence: ${confidence.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
      'timestamp: $timestamp)';
}
```

### Step 2: Implement StabilityScorer

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_scorer.dart`:

**Constructor parameters:**

```dart
class StabilityScorer {
  /// Number of [SensorFrame]s in the sliding window. Default: 30.
  /// At 30 Hz stream rate this represents ~1 second of history.
  final int windowSize;

  /// gyroMag variance threshold above which score reaches 0.0. Default: 0.5.
  /// Tune this constant — do not hardcode inline; keep as a named field.
  final double gyroVarianceMax;

  /// accelMag variance threshold above which score reaches 0.0. Default: 1.0.
  final double accelVarianceMax;

  /// Maximum angular delta (rad) between consecutive frames above which
  /// score reaches 0.0. Default: 0.2 rad (~11.5°).
  final double angularDeltaMax;
```

**Internal state:**

```dart
  final List<SensorFrame> _window = [];
```

**`add()` method — core algorithm:**

```dart
StabilityScore add(SensorFrame frame) {
  // 1. Append frame; evict oldest if window exceeds windowSize.
  _window.add(frame);
  if (_window.length > windowSize) _window.removeAt(0);

  // 2. Confidence = fraction of window that is filled.
  final confidence = _window.length / windowSize;

  // 3. Compute gyroMag variance over window.
  final gyroVar = _variance(_window.map((f) => f.gyroMag).toList());

  // 4. Compute accelMag variance over window.
  final accelVar = _variance(_window.map((f) => f.accelMag).toList());

  // 5. Compute mean angular delta between consecutive frame pairs.
  final angularDelta = _meanAngularDelta(_window);

  // 6. Map each signal to a partial score in [0.0, 1.0].
  //    Score is 1.0 when signal is 0, falls linearly to 0.0 at its max threshold.
  final gyroScore   = (1.0 - (gyroVar   / gyroVarianceMax).clamp(0.0, 1.0));
  final accelScore  = (1.0 - (accelVar  / accelVarianceMax).clamp(0.0, 1.0));
  final angleScore  = (1.0 - (angularDelta / angularDeltaMax).clamp(0.0, 1.0));

  // 7. Final score = geometric mean of three partial scores.
  //    Geometric mean punishes any single signal being very low
  //    harder than arithmetic mean would.
  final score = _geometricMean([gyroScore, accelScore, angleScore]);

  return StabilityScore(
    score: score,
    confidence: confidence,
    timestamp: frame.timestamp,
  );
}
```

**Private helpers — implement all three:**

```dart
/// Population variance of a list of doubles. Returns 0.0 for lists
/// with fewer than 2 elements (no variance computable).
double _variance(List<double> values) {
  if (values.length < 2) return 0.0;
  final mean = values.reduce((a, b) => a + b) / values.length;
  final sumSq = values.map((v) => (v - mean) * (v - mean)).reduce((a, b) => a + b);
  return sumSq / values.length;
}

/// Mean Euclidean angular delta between consecutive SensorFrame pairs
/// in the window. Returns 0.0 for windows with fewer than 2 frames.
/// Angular distance per pair = sqrt(Δyaw² + Δpitch² + Δroll²).
double _meanAngularDelta(List<SensorFrame> frames) {
  if (frames.length < 2) return 0.0;
  double total = 0.0;
  for (int i = 1; i < frames.length; i++) {
    final dy = frames[i].yaw   - frames[i - 1].yaw;
    final dp = frames[i].pitch - frames[i - 1].pitch;
    final dr = frames[i].roll  - frames[i - 1].roll;
    total += math.sqrt(dy * dy + dp * dp + dr * dr);
  }
  return total / (frames.length - 1);
}

/// Geometric mean of a list of values all in [0.0, 1.0].
/// Returns 0.0 if any input is 0.0 (product collapses).
double _geometricMean(List<double> values) {
  if (values.isEmpty) return 0.0;
  final product = values.reduce((a, b) => a * b);
  return math.pow(product, 1.0 / values.length).toDouble();
}
```

**`reset()` method:**

```dart
void reset() => _window.clear();
```

**Default constructor:**

```dart
StabilityScorer({
  this.windowSize     = 30,
  this.gyroVarianceMax  = 0.5,
  this.accelVarianceMax = 1.0,
  this.angularDeltaMax  = 0.2,
});
```

### Step 3: Write unit tests

Create `test/features/camera/sensor/stability_scorer_test.dart`.
Use `package:test` only — no widget test runner.

Define a helper at the top of the test file:

```dart
SensorFrame makeFrame({
  double yaw = 0.0,
  double pitch = 0.0,
  double roll = 0.0,
  double gyroMag = 0.0,
  double accelMag = 9.81,
  int timestamp = 1000,
}) => SensorFrame(
  yaw: yaw, pitch: pitch, roll: roll,
  gyroMag: gyroMag, accelMag: accelMag, timestamp: timestamp,
);
```

Implement ALL of the following test cases:

1. **Score = 1.0 for perfectly still frames**
   Feed `windowSize` identical frames (`gyroMag: 0, accelMag: 9.81`, no angular change).
   Assert `score ≈ 1.0` (within `1e-9`).

2. **Score approaches 0.0 under maximum motion**
   Feed `windowSize` frames where `gyroMag` alternates between `0` and `10.0`
   (variance >> `gyroVarianceMax`) and angular delta >> `angularDeltaMax`.
   Assert `score < 0.1`.

3. **Confidence ramp-up: first frame → confidence = 1/windowSize**
   Create scorer with `windowSize: 10`. Add one frame.
   Assert `confidence ≈ 0.1` (within `1e-9`).

4. **Confidence = 1.0 once window is full**
   Add exactly `windowSize` frames. Assert `confidence == 1.0`.

5. **Confidence does not exceed 1.0 after overflow**
   Add `windowSize + 5` frames. Assert `confidence == 1.0`.

6. **Window eviction: oldest frame is dropped after overflow**
   Create scorer with `windowSize: 3`. Add 4 frames with distinct timestamps.
   After the 4th `add()`, assert the internal `_window` has length 3 and the
   oldest timestamp is gone.
   Access `_window` via a test-only subclass or by exposing a
   `@visibleForTesting List<SensorFrame> get debugWindow => List.unmodifiable(_window);`
   getter on `StabilityScorer` (add this getter — it must not exist in the public API).

7. **reset() clears window**
   Add `windowSize` frames, call `reset()`, add one frame.
   Assert `confidence ≈ 1/windowSize` and `_window.length == 1`.

8. **Score is monotonically non-increasing as gyroMag increases**
   For `gyroMag` in `[0.0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0]`, create a fresh scorer
   for each, feed `windowSize` identical frames at that `gyroMag`, collect scores.
   Assert each successive score is ≤ the previous.

9. **timestamp matches most recent frame's timestamp**
   Add three frames with timestamps `1000`, `2000`, `3000`.
   Assert `StabilityScore.timestamp == 3000`.

10. **Single-frame window: variance = 0.0, no angular delta**
    Create scorer with `windowSize: 1`. Add one frame with `gyroMag: 0.5`.
    Assert `score ≈ 1.0` — single frame has zero variance and zero angular delta
    regardless of magnitude values, so all partial scores are 1.0.

11. **StabilityScore.empty sentinel values**
    Assert `StabilityScore.empty.score == 0.0`,
    `StabilityScore.empty.confidence == 0.0`,
    `StabilityScore.empty.timestamp == 0`.

12. **StabilityScore equality**
    Assert two `StabilityScore` instances with identical fields are `==`.

  are frozen; this task only adds new files

  or score files — `package:flutter/foundation.dart` and `dart:math` only
- Do NOT add smoothing, Kalman filtering, or low-pass filtering inside
  `StabilityScorer` — raw variance over the window is the intended algorithm;
  filtering belongs in a layer above
- Do NOT expose `_window` publicly except via the `@visibleForTesting debugWindow`
  getter added in Step 3

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Window has exactly 1 frame → `_variance` returns `0.0`, `_meanAngularDelta`
      returns `0.0`; score must be `1.0` — verify test case #10 covers this
- [ ] All `gyroMag` values in window are identical → variance is `0.0` → gyroScore
      is `1.0`; this is correct and expected for a motionless device
- [ ] `gyroVarianceMax`, `accelVarianceMax`, or `angularDeltaMax` configured as `0.0`
      by caller → division by zero in partial score computation; guard each division:
      if the threshold is `0.0`, treat the partial score as `0.0` (maximum penalty),
      not `Infinity` or `NaN`
- [ ] Consecutive frames with identical timestamps (duplicate delivery from EventChannel)
      → angular delta for that pair is `0.0`; this is safe and requires no special case
- [ ] `_geometricMean` receives a list where one value is exactly `0.0` → returns `0.0`
      immediately (product is zero); this is correct — one completely unstable signal
      collapses the total score to zero

---

## Constraints

- `StabilityScorer` must be instantiable with a custom `windowSize` of `1` without
  throwing — edge case used in tests
- All threshold parameters (`gyroVarianceMax`, `accelVarianceMax`, `angularDeltaMax`)
  must be `final` fields set via the constructor — never magic numbers inlined in
  the algorithm body
- `add()` must return a new `StabilityScore` on every call — it must not return
  `StabilityScore.empty` after the first frame is added, even if the window is
  not yet full
- The `debugWindow` getter must be annotated `@visibleForTesting` and return an
  unmodifiable view — callers outside tests must not mutate `_window` through it
- `score` and `confidence` in `StabilityScore` must always be in `[0.0, 1.0]` —
  the `.clamp()` calls in `add()` enforce this; add an `assert` in the
  `StabilityScore` constructor in debug mode:
  `assert(score >= 0.0 && score <= 1.0)`
  `assert(confidence >= 0.0 && confidence <= 1.0)`

## Assumptions

- Assumed: `SensorFrame` fields `gyroMag` and `accelMag` are always ≥ 0.0
  (enforced by `sqrt()` in `SensorFrame.fromRaw`) — `StabilityScorer` does not
  re-validate this; if negative values arrive, variance computation remains
  correct but physical interpretation is undefined
- Assumed: stream rate is approximately 30 Hz; the default `windowSize: 30`
  represents ~1 second of history — if the native EventChannel fires at a
  different rate, callers should pass a custom `windowSize` at construction time
- Assumed: no thread-safety requirement — `StabilityScorer` will be called from
  a single Dart isolate; do not add synchronisation primitives

  Generate a coding prompt for this task: Camera pitch band evaluator: isInPitchBand(level, pitch) → bool — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Flutter Platform Bridge

  # NEW FEATURE: Camera Pitch Band Evaluator — isInPitchBand(level, pitch) → bool
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Implement a pure-Dart `PitchBandEvaluator` that determines whether a device's current
pitch angle falls within the acceptable band for a named capture level. This is a
stateless, side-effect-free utility — no UI, no Riverpod, no I/O, no SensorFrame
history. It receives a `PitchLevel` enum and a `double pitch` (radians, sourced from
`SensorFrame.pitch`) and returns a `bool`.

- [ ] Define the `PitchLevel` enum with named capture levels, each carrying a
      centre angle and tolerance in radians
- [ ] Implement `PitchBandEvaluator` as a stateless class with a single public
      static method `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel level, double pitch) → bool`
- [ ] Implement `PitchBandEvaluator.bandFor(PitchLevel level) → PitchBand` —
      returns the raw band definition for a level, used by callers that need to
      display guidance UI (out of scope here, but the method must exist)
- [ ] Define the `PitchBand` immutable value type with `centre`, `tolerance`,
      and computed `lower` / `upper` bounds
- [ ] Write unit tests covering every `PitchLevel` at centre (must be `true`),
      at band edges (must be `true`), just outside edges (must be `false`),
      and at extreme pitch values

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_frame.dart` — confirm `SensorFrame.pitch`
   is in radians, range [-π/2, π/2]; `PitchBandEvaluator` consumes this field
   directly — do not modify `sensor_frame.dart`
2. `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_scorer.dart` — understand the existing
   stateless evaluator pattern to keep `PitchBandEvaluator` structurally consistent
3. `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_score.dart` — read value type conventions
   (`@immutable`, `const` constructor, `==`, `hashCode`, `toString`) to apply the
   same pattern to `PitchBand`
4. `test/features/camera/sensor/stability_scorer_test.dart` — read test file
   structure and `makeFrame` helper pattern; replicate the style in new tests
5. `pubspec.yaml` — confirm `dart:math` is sufficient; no new dependencies needed

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define PitchBand value type

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/pitch_band.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// Defines an inclusive angular band [lower, upper] around a [centre] pitch.
/// All values are in radians.
///
/// A device pitch is inside the band when:
///   lower <= pitch <= upper
/// which is equivalent to:
///   (pitch - centre).abs() <= tolerance
@immutable
class PitchBand {
  /// Centre of the acceptable pitch band. Radians.
  final double centre;

  /// Half-width of the band. Radians. Always > 0.
  final double tolerance;

  const PitchBand({
    required this.centre,
    required this.tolerance,
  }) : assert(tolerance > 0, 'tolerance must be positive');

  /// Inclusive lower bound. Radians.
  double get lower => centre - tolerance;

  /// Inclusive upper bound. Radians.
  double get upper => centre + tolerance;

  /// Returns true if [pitch] falls within [lower, upper] inclusive.
  bool contains(double pitch) => pitch >= lower && pitch <= upper;

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      identical(this, other) ||
      other is PitchBand &&
          centre == other.centre &&
          tolerance == other.tolerance;

  @override
  int get hashCode => Object.hash(centre, tolerance);

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'PitchBand(centre: ${centre.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
      'tolerance: ${tolerance.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
      'lower: ${lower.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
      'upper: ${upper.toStringAsFixed(4)})';
}
```

### Step 2: Define PitchLevel enum

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/pitch_level.dart`:

Define exactly these four levels with the bands below. All angles are in radians.
Do NOT use `dart:math.pi` expressions as enum field initialisers — Dart enums with
const fields require compile-time constants; use pre-computed literals instead.
Use the values given here verbatim (rounded to 6 decimal places):

```dart
/// Named capture levels, each mapping to a [PitchBand].
///
/// | Level     | Centre (rad) | Tolerance (rad) | Approx. angle |
/// |-----------|-------------|-----------------|---------------|
/// | overhead  | -0.785398   | 0.174533        | -45° ± 10°    |
/// | eye       |  0.000000   | 0.174533        |   0° ± 10°    |
/// | table     |  0.523599   | 0.174533        |  30° ± 10°    |
/// | ground    |  1.047198   | 0.261799        |  60° ± 15°    |
enum PitchLevel {
  overhead(centre: -0.785398, tolerance: 0.174533),
  eye(centre: 0.000000,  tolerance: 0.174533),
  table(centre: 0.523599,  tolerance: 0.174533),
  ground(centre: 1.047198,  tolerance: 0.261799);

  final double centre;
  final double tolerance;

  const PitchLevel({required this.centre, required this.tolerance});
}
```

If the product team adds a new capture level in future, it must be added only here —
not in `PitchBandEvaluator` or `PitchBand`.

### Step 3: Implement PitchBandEvaluator

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/pitch_band_evaluator.dart`:

```dart
import 'pitch_band.dart';
import 'pitch_level.dart';

/// Stateless evaluator that determines whether a pitch angle (radians)
/// falls within the acceptable band for a named [PitchLevel].
///
/// All methods are static — do not instantiate this class.
abstract final class PitchBandEvaluator {
  PitchBandEvaluator._();

  /// Returns true if [pitch] is within the band defined by [level].
  /// [pitch] must be in radians, sourced from [SensorFrame.pitch].
  static bool isInPitchBand(PitchLevel level, double pitch) =>
      bandFor(level).contains(pitch);

  /// Returns the [PitchBand] definition for [level].
  /// Callers needing to display guidance arrows or range indicators
  /// use this to obtain [PitchBand.lower], [PitchBand.upper], and
  /// [PitchBand.centre] without re-implementing band arithmetic.
  static PitchBand bandFor(PitchLevel level) => PitchBand(
        centre: level.centre,
        tolerance: level.tolerance,
      );
}
```

`abstract final class` with a private constructor prevents instantiation and
subclassing — correct pattern for a purely static utility in Dart 3.

### Step 4: Write unit tests

Create `test/features/camera/sensor/pitch_band_evaluator_test.dart`.
Use `package:test` only — no widget test runner.

Define these constants at the top of the test file for readability.
Do NOT use `dart:math` expressions directly in `expect` calls — pre-compute:

```dart
// Pre-computed pitch values used across tests (radians)
const double kOverheadCentre   = -0.785398;
const double kEyeCentre        =  0.000000;
const double kTableCentre      =  0.523599;
const double kGroundCentre     =  1.047198;
const double kStdTolerance     =  0.174533; // ±10° in radians
const double kGroundTolerance  =  0.261799; // ±15° in radians
const double kEpsilon          =  1e-6;     // test nudge past boundary
```

Implement ALL of the following test cases:

**PitchBand unit tests (group: 'PitchBand')**

1. **contains — pitch at centre returns true**
   `PitchBand(centre: 0.0, tolerance: 0.1).contains(0.0)` → `true`

2. **contains — pitch at lower bound (inclusive) returns true**
   `PitchBand(centre: 0.0, tolerance: 0.1).contains(-0.1)` → `true`

3. **contains — pitch at upper bound (inclusive) returns true**
   `PitchBand(centre: 0.0, tolerance: 0.1).contains(0.1)` → `true`

4. **contains — pitch just below lower bound returns false**
   `PitchBand(centre: 0.0, tolerance: 0.1).contains(-0.1 - kEpsilon)` → `false`

5. **contains — pitch just above upper bound returns false**
   `PitchBand(centre: 0.0, tolerance: 0.1).contains(0.1 + kEpsilon)` → `false`

6. **lower and upper computed correctly**
   Band `centre: 1.0, tolerance: 0.5` → `lower == 0.5`, `upper == 1.5`

7. **equality — two PitchBands with same fields are ==**

8. **assert fires for tolerance = 0.0** (debug mode only)
   Wrap in `expect(() => PitchBand(centre: 0.0, tolerance: 0.0), throwsA(anything))`
   — this only triggers in debug mode; note this in a comment.

**isInPitchBand tests (group: 'PitchBandEvaluator.isInPitchBand')**

For each of the four `PitchLevel` values, test:

9. **overhead — centre pitch returns true**
   `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel.overhead, kOverheadCentre)` → `true`

10. **overhead — lower edge (inclusive) returns true**
    `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel.overhead, kOverheadCentre - kStdTolerance)` → `true`

11. **overhead — upper edge (inclusive) returns true**
    `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel.overhead, kOverheadCentre + kStdTolerance)` → `true`

12. **overhead — just outside lower edge returns false**
    `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel.overhead, kOverheadCentre - kStdTolerance - kEpsilon)` → `false`

13. **overhead — just outside upper edge returns false**
    `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel.overhead, kOverheadCentre + kStdTolerance + kEpsilon)` → `false`

Repeat tests 9–13 for `PitchLevel.eye`, `PitchLevel.table`, and `PitchLevel.ground`
(substituting the correct centre and tolerance constants). Total: 25 test cases in
this group.

**Extreme value tests (group: 'PitchBandEvaluator — extremes')**

26. **pitch = π/2 (~1.5708) is outside all standard bands**
    Assert `isInPitchBand(level, 1.5708)` is `false` for `eye`, `overhead`, `table`.
    (`ground` upper bound is `1.047198 + 0.261799 = 1.308997` so `1.5708` is also
    outside — assert `false` for `ground` too.)

27. **pitch = -π/2 (~-1.5708) is outside all bands**
    Assert `false` for all four levels.

28. **pitch = double.nan — must return false, not throw**
    `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel.eye, double.nan)` → `false`
    (`double.nan >= x` evaluates to `false` in Dart — no special case needed in
    implementation, but the test documents and locks this behaviour.)

29. **pitch = double.infinity — must return false, not throw**
    `isInPitchBand(PitchLevel.eye, double.infinity)` → `false`

**bandFor tests (group: 'PitchBandEvaluator.bandFor')**

30. **bandFor returns PitchBand with correct centre and tolerance for each level**
    Assert `bandFor(PitchLevel.table).centre == kTableCentre` and
    `bandFor(PitchLevel.table).tolerance == kStdTolerance`.
    Repeat for all four levels.

  `stability_score.dart`, or any of their tests

  no instance fields, no caching, no memoisation
- Do NOT create a Riverpod provider in this task — provider wiring is a
  separate task
- Do NOT import `package:flutter/material.dart` or any widget library in any
  new file — `package:flutter/foundation.dart` only (for `@immutable`)
- Do NOT rename `PitchLevel` enum cases — they are referenced by name in
  downstream task prompts (`overhead`, `eye`, `table`, `ground`)
- Do NOT change the band definitions (centre/tolerance values) in `PitchLevel`
  without explicit sign-off — they encode product-level capture UX requirements

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `pitch = double.nan` → `PitchBand.contains` returns `false` naturally
      because `nan >= lower` evaluates `false` in Dart; no guard needed in
      implementation but test case #28 must lock this behaviour
- [ ] `pitch = double.infinity` → same IEEE 754 behaviour; `false` naturally;
      test case #29 locks it
- [ ] `tolerance = 0.0` passed to `PitchBand` constructor → `assert` fires in
      debug mode; in release mode the band degenerates to a single point
      (`lower == upper == centre`); `contains` still returns a valid `bool` —
      do not throw in release mode
- [ ] Two adjacent `PitchLevel` bands overlapping (e.g. `table` upper =
      `0.523599 + 0.174533 = 0.698132`; `ground` lower =
      `1.047198 - 0.261799 = 0.785399`) — they do not overlap with current
      values; if band definitions change and they do overlap,
      `isInPitchBand` returning `true` for two levels simultaneously is
      correct and expected — evaluator makes no uniqueness guarantee

---

## Constraints

- `PitchBandEvaluator` must be `abstract final class` with a private `._()` 
  constructor — this is the correct Dart 3 pattern for a static-only utility;
  do not use a regular class with a factory or a top-level function
- `PitchLevel` enum values must use Dart enhanced enum syntax (constructor with
  named parameters) — not a separate `Map<PitchLevel, PitchBand>` lookup table
- All angle literals in `PitchLevel` must be pre-computed decimal constants,
  not `dart:math` expressions — Dart enhanced enum fields must be compile-time
  constants and `math.pi / 4` is not a `const` expression in this context
- `isInPitchBand` and `bandFor` must not allocate more than one `PitchBand`
  object per call — the current implementation satisfies this; do not add caching
  that would complicate the stateless guarantee
- `PitchBand.contains` must use inclusive comparison (`>=` and `<=`) at both
  bounds — exclusive bounds would fail the edge tests at exactly `lower`/`upper`

  `sensor_frame.dart` — `PitchBandEvaluator` does not clamp or validate the
  incoming pitch; out-of-range values from a broken sensor will simply return
  `false` for all bands, which is the correct failure mode
- Assumed: the four `PitchLevel` definitions (`overhead`, `eye`, `table`,
  `ground`) are final for this sprint — if product requires additional levels
  (e.g. `shelf` at 15°), they are added to the `PitchLevel` enum only; no
  other files change
- Assumed: `isInPitchBand` is called on the Dart UI isolate at frame rate
  (~30–60 Hz); the implementation is allocation-light enough that no
  performance concern arises — if called from a compute isolate in future,
  no changes are needed since the class is stateless

  Generate a coding prompt for this task: Blur score + exposure check results passed from native → Dart via MethodChannel result — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Flutter Platform Bridge

  # NEW FEATURE: Blur Score + Exposure Check — Native → Dart MethodChannel Bridge
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

## Task Description

Implement the full bridge that carries blur score and exposure check results from
native image analysis (Android/iOS) into Dart as typed value objects. This task
covers three layers: the Dart-side value types, the MethodChannel invocation
wrapper, and the parser that converts the raw platform Map into typed models.
Native implementation is out of scope — the native handler is assumed to exist;
this task wires only the Dart side.

- [ ] Define `BlurResult` immutable value type: `score` (double), `isBlurry` (bool),
      `timestamp` (int)
- [ ] Define `ExposureResult` immutable value type: `meanLuminance` (double),
      `isUnderexposed` (bool), `isOverexposed` (bool), `timestamp` (int)
- [ ] Define `FrameQualityResult` immutable value type that composes both:
      `blur` (`BlurResult`), `exposure` (`ExposureResult`), `timestamp` (int),
      and computed `isAcceptable` (bool)
- [ ] Implement `FrameQualityChannel` — the MethodChannel wrapper class with a
      single public method `Future<FrameQualityResult> evaluate(String imagePath)`
      that invokes the native method and parses the response
- [ ] Implement `FrameQualityParser` — the static parser class that converts
      the raw `Map<dynamic, dynamic>` returned by the platform into
      `FrameQualityResult`, throwing `FrameQualityParseException` on failure
- [ ] Write unit tests covering parser happy path, all missing-key permutations,
      wrong-type fields, `isAcceptable` logic, and MethodChannel mock invocation

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define FrameQualityParseException

Create `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_parse_exception.dart`:

Model this exactly on `SensorParseException` in `sensor_frame.dart`:

```dart
class FrameQualityParseException implements Exception {
  final String message;
  final Map<dynamic, dynamic>? rawPayload;

  const FrameQualityParseException(this.message, {this.rawPayload});

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'FrameQualityParseException: $message | raw: $rawPayload';
}
```

### Step 2: Define BlurResult

Create `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/blur_result.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// Result of native blur detection on a single captured frame.
///
/// [score] ∈ [0.0, ∞). Lower = blurrier. Threshold for [isBlurry] is
/// determined natively — Dart does not recompute it.
/// [timestamp] is microseconds since Unix epoch, matching the frame
/// that was analysed.
@immutable
class BlurResult {
  final double score;
  final bool isBlurry;
  final int timestamp;

  const BlurResult({
    required this.score,
    required this.isBlurry,
    required this.timestamp,
  }) : assert(score >= 0.0, 'blur score must be non-negative');

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      identical(this, other) ||
      other is BlurResult &&
          score == other.score &&
          isBlurry == other.isBlurry &&
          timestamp == other.timestamp;

  @override
  int get hashCode => Object.hash(score, isBlurry, timestamp);

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'BlurResult(score: ${score.toStringAsFixed(4)}, '
      'isBlurry: $isBlurry, timestamp: $timestamp)';
}
```

### Step 3: Define ExposureResult

Create `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/exposure_result.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// Result of native exposure analysis on a single captured frame.
///
/// [meanLuminance] ∈ [0.0, 255.0] — 8-bit luminance scale.
/// [isUnderexposed] and [isOverexposed] are mutually exclusive;
/// both false means exposure is acceptable.
/// [timestamp] is microseconds since Unix epoch.
@immutable
class ExposureResult {
  final double meanLuminance;
  final bool isUnderexposed;
  final bool isOverexposed;
  final int timestamp;

  const ExposureResult({
    required this.meanLuminance,
    required this.isUnderexposed,
    required this.isOverexposed,
    required this.timestamp,
  })  : assert(meanLuminance >= 0.0 && meanLuminance <= 255.0,
            'meanLuminance must be in [0.0, 255.0]'),
        assert(!(isUnderexposed && isOverexposed),
            'isUnderexposed and isOverexposed cannot both be true');

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      identical(this, other) ||
      other is ExposureResult &&
          meanLuminance == other.meanLuminance &&
          isUnderexposed == other.isUnderexposed &&
          isOverexposed == other.isOverexposed &&
          timestamp == other.timestamp;

  @override
  int get hashCode =>
      Object.hash(meanLuminance, isUnderexposed, isOverexposed, timestamp);

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'ExposureResult(meanLuminance: ${meanLuminance.toStringAsFixed(2)}, '
      'isUnderexposed: $isUnderexposed, isOverexposed: $isOverexposed, '
      'timestamp: $timestamp)';
}
```

### Step 4: Define FrameQualityResult

Create `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_result.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'blur_result.dart';
import 'exposure_result.dart';

/// Composed quality assessment for a single captured frame.
///
/// [isAcceptable] is true only when the frame is not blurry AND
/// exposure is neither under nor over — both conditions must hold.
/// [timestamp] matches the native frame timestamp (microseconds since epoch)
/// and must equal both [blur.timestamp] and [exposure.timestamp].
@immutable
class FrameQualityResult {
  final BlurResult blur;
  final ExposureResult exposure;
  final int timestamp;

  const FrameQualityResult({
    required this.blur,
    required this.exposure,
    required this.timestamp,
  });

  /// True only when frame is sharp AND exposure is acceptable.
  bool get isAcceptable =>
      !blur.isBlurry &&
      !exposure.isUnderexposed &&
      !exposure.isOverexposed;

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      identical(this, other) ||
      other is FrameQualityResult &&
          blur == other.blur &&
          exposure == other.exposure &&
          timestamp == other.timestamp;

  @override
  int get hashCode => Object.hash(blur, exposure, timestamp);

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'FrameQualityResult(blur: $blur, exposure: $exposure, '
      'timestamp: $timestamp, isAcceptable: $isAcceptable)';
}
```

### Step 5: Implement FrameQualityParser

Create `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_parser.dart`:

The native MethodChannel returns a `Map<dynamic, dynamic>` with this exact shape
(read from native handlers in Step 0 above — confirm keys match before writing):
{

"blur_score":       double | int,   // required

"is_blurry":        bool,           // required

"mean_luminance":   double | int,   // required

"is_underexposed":  bool,           // required

"is_overexposed":   bool,           // required

"timestamp_us":     int,            // required — single timestamp for

//   the whole result; applied to

//   blur, exposure, and root

}

Unknown keys must be silently ignored for forward-compatibility.

```dart
import 'blur_result.dart';
import 'exposure_result.dart';
import 'frame_quality_parse_exception.dart';
import 'frame_quality_result.dart';

abstract final class FrameQualityParser {
  FrameQualityParser._();

  static FrameQualityResult parse(dynamic raw) {
    if (raw is! Map) {
      throw FrameQualityParseException(
        'Expected Map from MethodChannel, got ${raw.runtimeType}',
      );
    }
    final map = Map<dynamic, dynamic>.from(raw as Map);

    final timestamp = _requireInt(map, 'timestamp_us');

    final blur = BlurResult(
      score:     _requireDouble(map, 'blur_score'),
      isBlurry:  _requireBool(map, 'is_blurry'),
      timestamp: timestamp,
    );

    final exposure = ExposureResult(
      meanLuminance:  _requireDouble(map, 'mean_luminance'),
      isUnderexposed: _requireBool(map, 'is_underexposed'),
      isOverexposed:  _requireBool(map, 'is_overexposed'),
      timestamp:      timestamp,
    );

    return FrameQualityResult(
      blur:      blur,
      exposure:  exposure,
      timestamp: timestamp,
    );
  }

  static int _requireInt(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw, String key) {
    final v = raw[key];
    if (v == null) throw FrameQualityParseException(
        'Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (v is int) return v;
    if (v is num) return v.toInt();
    throw FrameQualityParseException(
        'Expected int for $key, got ${v.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  static double _requireDouble(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw, String key) {
    final v = raw[key];
    if (v == null) throw FrameQualityParseException(
        'Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (v is double) return v;
    if (v is num) return v.toDouble();
    throw FrameQualityParseException(
        'Expected double for $key, got ${v.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  static bool _requireBool(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw, String key) {
    final v = raw[key];
    if (v == null) throw FrameQualityParseException(
        'Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (v is bool) return v;
    throw FrameQualityParseException(
        'Expected bool for $key, got ${v.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }
}
```

### Step 6: Implement FrameQualityChannel

Create `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_channel.dart`:

The channel name must exactly match the native registration confirmed in
"Files to Inspect First" step 4/5. Placeholder used below — replace with the
actual string found in native code:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';
import 'frame_quality_parse_exception.dart';
import 'frame_quality_parser.dart';
import 'frame_quality_result.dart';

class FrameQualityChannel {
  static const _channelName = 'com.mayasabhaxr.camera/frame_quality';

  final MethodChannel _channel;

  FrameQualityChannel({MethodChannel? channel})
      : _channel = channel ??
            const MethodChannel(_channelName);

  /// Invokes native blur + exposure analysis on the frame at [imagePath].
  ///
  /// [imagePath] must be an absolute file path to a JPEG written by
  /// [CaptureController.triggerCapture()] — do not pass a relative path
  /// or an asset URI.
  ///
  /// Throws [FrameQualityParseException] if the native response cannot
  /// be parsed.
  /// Throws [PlatformException] if the native side returns an error code
  /// (e.g. file not found, analysis timeout) — callers must handle both.
  Future<FrameQualityResult> evaluate(String imagePath) async {
    final result = await _channel.invokeMethod<dynamic>(
      'evaluateFrameQuality',
      {'image_path': imagePath},
    );
    return FrameQualityParser.parse(result);
  }
}
```

### Step 7: Write unit tests

Create `test/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_test.dart`.
Use `package:test` and `package:flutter_test` (needed for
`TestDefaultBinaryMessengerBinding` to mock MethodChannel).

Define a valid base fixture at the top of the file:

```dart
const kValidRaw = <dynamic, dynamic>{
  'blur_score':      12.5,
  'is_blurry':       false,
  'mean_luminance':  128.0,
  'is_underexposed': false,
  'is_overexposed':  false,
  'timestamp_us':    1000000,
};
```

Implement ALL of the following test cases:

**FrameQualityParser tests (group: 'FrameQualityParser')**

1. **Valid full payload → correct BlurResult fields**
   Assert `result.blur.score == 12.5`, `result.blur.isBlurry == false`,
   `result.blur.timestamp == 1000000`.

2. **Valid full payload → correct ExposureResult fields**
   Assert `result.exposure.meanLuminance == 128.0`,
   `result.exposure.isUnderexposed == false`,
   `result.exposure.isOverexposed == false`.

3. **Valid full payload → timestamp propagated to all three levels**
   Assert `result.timestamp == result.blur.timestamp
       == result.exposure.timestamp == 1000000`.

4. **isAcceptable = true when not blurry and exposure acceptable**
   Use `kValidRaw` → assert `result.isAcceptable == true`.

5. **isAcceptable = false when isBlurry = true**
   Override `is_blurry: true` → assert `result.isAcceptable == false`.

6. **isAcceptable = false when isUnderexposed = true**
   Override `is_underexposed: true` → assert `result.isAcceptable == false`.

7. **isAcceptable = false when isOverexposed = true**
   Override `is_overexposed: true` → assert `result.isAcceptable == false`.

8. **isAcceptable = false when blurry AND underexposed simultaneously**
   Override both → assert `false`.

9. **Missing key 'blur_score' → FrameQualityParseException with 'blur_score'**

10. **Missing key 'is_blurry' → FrameQualityParseException with 'is_blurry'**

11. **Missing key 'mean_luminance' → FrameQualityParseException**

12. **Missing key 'is_underexposed' → FrameQualityParseException**

13. **Missing key 'is_overexposed' → FrameQualityParseException**

14. **Missing key 'timestamp_us' → FrameQualityParseException**

15. **Wrong type for 'is_blurry' (String instead of bool) → FrameQualityParseException**
    Pass `'is_blurry': 'yes'` → assert throws with message containing `'is_blurry'`.

16. **Wrong type for 'blur_score' (String) → FrameQualityParseException**

17. **int value for 'blur_score' is coerced to double without throwing**
    Pass `'blur_score': 12` (int) → assert `result.blur.score == 12.0`.

18. **int value for 'mean_luminance' is coerced to double without throwing**

19. **Unknown extra key in payload is silently ignored**
    Add `'future_field': 'ignored'` to `kValidRaw` → no exception, result parses normally.

20. **Non-Map input (raw String) → FrameQualityParseException**
    `FrameQualityParser.parse('not a map')` → throws `FrameQualityParseException`.

21. **Null input → FrameQualityParseException**
    `FrameQualityParser.parse(null)` → throws `FrameQualityParseException`.

**FrameQualityResult / value type tests (group: 'FrameQualityResult')**

22. **Equality — two results with identical fields are ==**

23. **Equality — results differing by blur.score are !=**

24. **toString contains 'isAcceptable'**
    Assert `result.toString()` contains the substring `'isAcceptable'`.

**FrameQualityChannel tests (group: 'FrameQualityChannel')**

25. **evaluate() returns correctly parsed FrameQualityResult on success**
    Mock the MethodChannel using `TestDefaultBinaryMessengerBinding` to return
    `kValidRaw` for method `'evaluateFrameQuality'`.
    Assert `result.blur.score == 12.5` and `result.isAcceptable == true`.

26. **evaluate() passes imagePath as 'image_path' argument to native**
    Capture the arguments map from the mock handler.
    Assert `args['image_path'] == '/tmp/test_frame.jpg'`.

27. **evaluate() propagates PlatformException from native without wrapping**
    Mock handler throws `PlatformException(code: 'FILE_NOT_FOUND')`.
    Assert `evaluate()` throws `PlatformException` (not `FrameQualityParseException`).

28. **evaluate() throws FrameQualityParseException when native returns malformed Map**
    Mock handler returns `{'bad_key': 1}`.
    Assert `evaluate()` throws `FrameQualityParseException`.

---

## API / Data Contract

Native → Dart MethodChannel payload (`Map<dynamic, dynamic>`):
Method name:  'evaluateFrameQuality'

Argument in:  { "image_path": String }  // absolute JPEG file path
Response out:

{

"blur_score":       double | int,  // required — Laplacian variance; higher = sharper

"is_blurry":        bool,          // required — native threshold applied

"mean_luminance":   double | int,  // required — 8-bit mean; range [0.0, 255.0]

"is_underexposed":  bool,          // required

"is_overexposed":   bool,          // required

"timestamp_us":     int,           // required — microseconds since Unix epoch

}

Error codes the native side may return as `PlatformException.code`:
- `'FILE_NOT_FOUND'` — `imagePath` does not exist on disk
- `'ANALYSIS_TIMEOUT'` — native analysis exceeded time budget
- `'UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT'` — file is not a valid JPEG

Dart callers of `FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()` must handle both
`FrameQualityParseException` and `PlatformException` — document this in
the method's dartdoc comment (already included in Step 6).

---

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify any native platform files (Kotlin, Swift, ObjC) — Dart-only task
- Do NOT modify `sensor_frame.dart`, `stability_scorer.dart`,
  `pitch_band_evaluator.dart`, or any of their tests
- Do NOT create a Riverpod provider in this task — provider wiring is a
  separate task
- Do NOT import `package:flutter/material.dart` in value type or parser files —
  `package:flutter/foundation.dart` and `package:flutter/services.dart` only
- Do NOT add retry logic or caching inside `FrameQualityChannel` — it is a
  thin transport wrapper; retry policy belongs in the caller
- Do NOT recompute `isBlurry` or exposure flags in Dart — these are computed
  natively and passed as booleans; the Dart layer trusts them as-is

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Native returns `null` for the entire result (channel teardown or null
      return from handler) → `FrameQualityParser.parse(null)` throws
      `FrameQualityParseException`; test case #21 covers this
- [ ] `blur_score` arrives as `int` from Android (Kotlin `Int` serialises
      as `int` across the StandardMessageCodec) → `_requireDouble` coerces
      via `.toDouble()`; test case #17 covers this
- [ ] `mean_luminance` arrives as `int` → same coercion; test case #18 covers this
- [ ] Both `is_underexposed` and `is_overexposed` are `true` simultaneously →
      `ExposureResult` `assert` fires in debug mode; in release mode the object
      is constructed and `isAcceptable` is `false`; the native side must never
      produce this state — note with a `// INVARIANT:` comment in `ExposureResult`
- [ ] `mean_luminance` outside [0.0, 255.0] (hardware glitch) → `assert` fires
      in debug; release mode stores the value as-is; no clamping in Dart —
      the native side owns range validity
- [ ] `imagePath` is an empty string passed to `evaluate()` → do NOT validate
      in `FrameQualityChannel`; pass through to native and let the
      `'FILE_NOT_FOUND'` `PlatformException` surface naturally

---

## Constraints

- The MethodChannel name `'com.mayasabhaxr.camera/frame_quality'` is a
  placeholder — replace with the exact string read from native source in
  "Files to Inspect First"; the string must appear in exactly one place
  (the `_channelName` constant in `FrameQualityChannel`)
- `FrameQualityParser` must be `abstract final class` with a private `._()` 
  constructor — same pattern as `PitchBandEvaluator`; all methods are static
- `MethodChannel` must be constructor-injected into `FrameQualityChannel` so
  unit tests can supply a mock without `flutter_test` platform channel setup
  overriding global state
- All three value types (`BlurResult`, `ExposureResult`, `FrameQualityResult`)
  must be `const`-constructible via their primary constructors
- `isAcceptable` must be a computed getter — not a stored field — so it always
  reflects the current state of `blur` and `exposure` without a sync risk

---

## Assumptions

- Assumed: the native MethodChannel handler for `'evaluateFrameQuality'`
  already exists and returns the Map shape specified in the Data Contract —
  if the native implementation is not yet complete, mock it in the test using
  `TestDefaultBinaryMessengerBinding` (as in test cases #25–28) and proceed;
  the channel name must still be confirmed against native source before merging
- Assumed: `timestamp_us` in the native response is a single value shared by
  both blur and exposure results (they analyse the same frame) — if native
  returns separate timestamps per sub-result, the Map contract and parser must
  be updated before this prompt is executed; raise with the native team first
- Assumed: `blur_score` uses Laplacian variance as the sharpness metric
  (unbounded above, 0 = uniform/blurry) — the Dart layer stores it as-is with
  no upper bound clamping; if the native metric changes (e.g. to a normalised
  [0,1] scale), only the `assert` in `BlurResult` needs updating

# QA: Test Blur Detection Thresholds Across 5+ Device Types
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: QA
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Validate that the native blur detection threshold — the `is_blurry` boolean
returned via `FrameQualityChannel` — produces correct classifications across
a minimum of five distinct physical device types covering low-end Android,
mid-range Android, flagship Android, and iOS. The threshold is set natively;
this task does not change any production code. It produces a structured test
report, a threshold calibration recommendation, and failing-device bug tickets
if misclassification is found.

- [ ] Capture a controlled test image set (sharp, motion-blurred, defocus-blurred,
      borderline) on each device and run them through `FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()`
- [ ] Record `blur_score` and `is_blurry` for every image on every device
- [ ] Identify threshold inconsistency: cases where identical input images produce
      different `is_blurry` classifications across devices
- [ ] Identify misclassification: sharp images flagged as blurry, or blurry images
      passed as sharp
- [ ] Produce a `blur_threshold_report.md` with per-device findings, score
      distributions, and a pass/fail verdict per device
- [ ] File a bug ticket (inline in the report) for each device that fails
      the acceptance criteria

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_channel.dart` — understand
   `evaluate(String imagePath)` signature, `PlatformException` error codes, and
   the `MethodChannel` name; this is the only production entry point used during testing
2. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/blur_result.dart` — confirm `score` field
   semantics (higher = sharper, Laplacian variance, unbounded above) and `isBlurry`
   field; tests read these fields directly
3. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_result.dart` — confirm
   `isAcceptable` composition logic; verify blur contributes correctly
4. `android/app/src/main/kotlin/` — locate the native blur threshold constant
   (the value compared against `blur_score` to set `is_blurry`); record it in the
   report; do NOT change it
5. `ios/Runner/` — locate the equivalent iOS blur threshold constant; record it;
   confirm whether Android and iOS use the same numeric threshold or different ones
6. `test/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_test.dart` — read existing
   mock-based unit tests to understand what is already covered; this QA task covers
   what unit tests cannot: real hardware variance

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Prepare the test image corpus

Create `test/qa/blur_threshold/test_images/` and populate with exactly these
four categories. Use a tripod or fixed mount to eliminate operator shake:

**Category A — Sharp (ground truth: isBlurry must be false)**
- `sharp_near.jpg` — subject at 30 cm, full focus, bright indoor light
- `sharp_far.jpg` — subject at 150 cm, full focus, bright indoor light
- `sharp_low_light.jpg` — subject at 50 cm, full focus, dim indoor light (~50 lux)

**Category B — Motion blur (ground truth: isBlurry must be true)**
- `motion_fast.jpg` — subject moving at arm speed during capture (deliberate shake)
- `motion_slow.jpg` — slow pan during capture (~5 cm/s lateral movement)

**Category C — Defocus blur (ground truth: isBlurry must be true)**
- `defocus_severe.jpg` — lens deliberately out of focus by 5+ diopters
- `defocus_mild.jpg` — lens slightly out of focus (borderline — document actual
  result, do not assert a fixed expectation for this image)

**Category D — Borderline (ground truth: document only, no pass/fail assertion)**
- `borderline_01.jpg` — slight camera shake, handheld at 1/30s equivalent
- `borderline_02.jpg` — subject partially in focus, partially soft

Store the same physical image set on all test devices using `adb push` (Android)
or Xcode device file transfer (iOS) to `<app_documents_dir>/qa_images/`.
All devices must analyse the exact same JPEG bytes — do not re-shoot per device.

### Step 2: Build and run the QA test harness

Create `test/qa/blur_threshold/blur_threshold_harness.dart` as a Flutter
integration test (use `package:integration_test`).

The harness must:

```dart
// For each image path in the corpus:
final result = await FrameQualityChannel().evaluate(imagePath);
// Record: imageName, blur_score, is_blurry, device model, OS version,
//         expected classification (from category above), match (bool)
```

Collect all records into a `List<Map<String, dynamic>>` and write to
`/sdcard/qa_output/blur_results_<deviceModel>.json` on Android or
the app documents directory on iOS using `dart:io` `File.writeAsString()`.

Do NOT use `flutter_test` `expect()` calls inside the harness loop for
category D (borderline) images — collect and report only.
DO assert for categories A, B, and C after the loop completes:

```dart
// After all images processed:
final failures = records.where((r) =>
    r['category'] != 'D' && r['match'] == false).toList();
expect(failures, isEmpty,
    reason: 'Misclassifications found: ${jsonEncode(failures)}');
```

### Step 3: Execute on each required device

Run the harness on a minimum of five devices from this required spread.
Substitute with nearest available device if exact model unavailable —
document the substitution in the report:

| Slot | Target device            | Category          | Min Android/iOS |
|------|--------------------------|-------------------|-----------------|
| D1   | Redmi Note 10 / 10S      | Low-end Android   | Android 11      |
| D2   | Samsung Galaxy A34       | Mid-range Android | Android 13      |
| D3   | Samsung Galaxy S23       | Flagship Android  | Android 13      |
| D4   | Google Pixel 7           | Stock Android     | Android 13      |
| D5   | iPhone 13 or 14          | Mid-range iOS     | iOS 16          |
| D6   | (optional) iPhone SE 3   | Low-end iOS       | iOS 16          |

Run command per Android device:
flutter drive 

--driver=test_driver/integration_test.dart 

--target=test/qa/blur_threshold/blur_threshold_harness.dart 

--device-id=<DEVICE_SERIAL>

Pull output after each run:
adb -s <DEVICE_SERIAL> pull /sdcard/qa_output/blur_results_<deviceModel>.json 

test/qa/blur_threshold/results/

For iOS: retrieve the JSON from the app documents directory via Xcode Devices
window → Download Container → App Data → Documents.

### Step 4: Analyse results and write the report

Create `test/qa/blur_threshold/blur_threshold_report.md`.

The report must contain exactly these sections:

**1. Executive Summary**
One paragraph. State: number of devices tested, total images evaluated,
total misclassifications found, overall pass/fail verdict.

**2. Native Threshold Constants**
Table with columns: Platform | File | Constant name | Value.
Record the exact threshold values read from native source in "Files to Inspect First".
If Android and iOS differ, flag this explicitly.

**3. Per-Device Results**
For each device, a sub-section containing:
- Device model, OS version, test date
- Table: Image name | Category | blur_score | is_blurry | Expected | Match
- Per-device verdict: PASS or FAIL
- If FAIL: list specific misclassified images with scores

**4. Score Distribution Analysis**
For each category (A, B, C) across all devices combined:
- Min, max, mean, median `blur_score`
- Overlap analysis: do category A scores ever overlap with category B/C scores?
  If yes, identify the overlap range — this is the threshold danger zone.

**5. Cross-Device Consistency**
Table: Image name | D1 score | D2 score | D3 score | D4 score | D5 score |
       Score range (max−min) | is_blurry consistent? (bool)

Flag any image where `is_blurry` differs across devices for the same JPEG bytes.
This indicates a platform-specific threshold or image-processing difference that
must be resolved before shipping.

**6. Borderline Image Findings**
Document `blur_score` for `borderline_01.jpg` and `borderline_02.jpg` on each device.
State whether they are near the threshold and whether classification is consistent.

**7. Threshold Calibration Recommendation**
Based on the score distributions in section 4:
- State the current threshold (from section 2)
- State the recommended threshold range that maximises separation between
  category A and category B/C with ≥ 95% accuracy
- If Android and iOS thresholds should differ, state both recommended values
- If the current threshold is already within the recommended range, state: "No change required"

**8. Bug Tickets**
For each device that fails, include a structured bug ticket:
BUG-QA-BLUR-<N>
Device: <model, OS>

Severity: High / Medium (High if > 2 misclassifications; Medium if 1–2)

Summary: Blur detection misclassifies <image_name> on <device>

Expected: is_blurry = <true/false>

Actual: is_blurry = <true/false>, blur_score = <value>

Steps to reproduce:

Install maya-viewer debug build <commit hash>
Push <image_name>.jpg to device
Call FrameQualityChannel.evaluate() with that path
Observe is_blurry = <actual>

Proposed fix: Adjust native threshold from <current> to <recommended>

on <platform>


### Step 5: Commit outputs

Commit to the branch:
- `test/qa/blur_threshold/blur_threshold_harness.dart`
- `test/qa/blur_threshold/results/blur_results_<deviceModel>.json` × N devices
- `test/qa/blur_threshold/blur_threshold_report.md`
- `test/qa/blur_threshold/test_images/` — include the 9 JPEG files used

Do NOT commit any changes to production source files.

## What NOT to Change

  task documents the current threshold behaviour; threshold changes require a

- Do NOT modify `frame_quality_channel.dart`, `blur_result.dart`,
  `frame_quality_result.dart`, or any production Dart source
- Do NOT modify existing unit tests in `frame_quality_test.dart`
- Do NOT add new Riverpod providers or wire the QA harness into the app
  navigation — it is a standalone integration test only

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()` throws `PlatformException` with code
      `'FILE_NOT_FOUND'` → log the error in the JSON output as
      `{ "error": "FILE_NOT_FOUND", "image": "<name>" }`, skip the record for
      pass/fail counting, and continue the harness loop — do not crash the test run
- [ ] `FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()` throws `PlatformException` with code
      `'ANALYSIS_TIMEOUT'` → same handling as above; flag in the report under
      the relevant device section
- [ ] Device storage path for `qa_images/` is not writable (permission denied
      on Android 13+ scoped storage) → use `path_provider`'s
      `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()` as fallback; document the actual
      path used in the report
- [ ] `blur_score` for a category A image is below the native threshold on one
      device but not others (same JPEG bytes) → this is a hardware codec or
      ISP preprocessing difference; flag in section 5 (Cross-Device Consistency)
      and section 8 (Bug Tickets) of the report
- [ ] Fewer than 5 physical devices available → test on all available; document
      missing slots in the report with reason; mark the overall verdict as
      INCOMPLETE rather than PASS or FAIL

---

## Constraints

- The test image corpus must be identical across all devices — same JPEG bytes,
  not re-shot per device; use `adb push` / Xcode transfer to ensure this
- `blur_threshold_harness.dart` must use `package:integration_test` — not
  `package:flutter_test` widget tests — because it requires a real
  `MethodChannel` connection to the native analysis pipeline
- JSON output filenames must follow `blur_results_<deviceModel>.json` exactly,
  where `<deviceModel>` is the value returned by
  `Platform.isAndroid ? androidInfo.model : iosInfo.model` (use
  `package:device_info_plus`) — do not use manual strings
- The report's score tables must show raw `blur_score` values to 4 decimal
  places — do not round to integers, as threshold proximity analysis requires
  decimal precision
- Category D (borderline) images must never cause the harness to fail the
  integration test — they are observation-only

## Testing Instructions

adb -s <SERIAL> shell mkdir -p /sdcard/qa_images/
