Generate a coding prompt for this task: Test IMU stream stability gate: fire shutter mid-motion → reject — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>>>>>>>

# QA: Test IMU Stream Stability Gate — Fire Shutter Mid-Motion → Reject
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: QA
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Validate that the stability gate — the combined pipeline of `SensorStreamParser`,
`StabilityScorer`, and `FrameQualityChannel` — correctly rejects a capture
attempt when the device is in motion at shutter-fire time. A rejected capture
means `StabilityScore.score` is below the acceptance threshold AND
`FrameQualityResult.isAcceptable` is `false` or the capture is suppressed
before reaching the native shutter. This task covers both unit-level injection
tests (simulated motion via fake `SensorFrame` streams) and physical device
integration tests (real IMU motion during shutter fire).

- [ ] Write unit tests that inject mid-motion `SensorFrame` sequences into
      `StabilityScorer` and assert score falls below the rejection threshold
- [ ] Write unit tests that confirm a capture triggered during a low-score
      window produces `FrameQualityResult.isAcceptable == false` when blurry
      frames are also returned by the mocked `FrameQualityChannel`
- [ ] Write integration tests that physically shake the device during shutter
      fire and assert the stability gate rejects the capture
- [ ] Write integration tests that hold the device still, fire the shutter,
      and assert the stability gate accepts the capture (regression guard)
- [ ] Produce a `stability_gate_report.md` documenting score distributions
      under motion and stillness, threshold adequacy, and per-device findings
      across 3+ physical devices

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_scorer.dart` — read `windowSize`,
   `gyroVarianceMax`, `accelVarianceMax`, `angularDeltaMax` default values,
   and the `add(SensorFrame) → StabilityScore` method; the unit tests drive
   this class directly with injected frames
2. `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_score.dart` — confirm `score` range
   [0.0, 1.0] and `confidence` semantics; identify what score value constitutes
   a rejection — there is currently no named threshold constant; you will
   define `kStabilityRejectionThreshold` as a test-local constant (do NOT
   add it to production code in this task)
3. `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_frame.dart` — read `SensorFrame`
   constructor fields (`yaw`, `pitch`, `roll`, `gyroMag`, `accelMag`,
   `timestamp`) to build motion and stillness fixture factories
4. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_channel.dart` — read
   `evaluate(String imagePath)` and how `PlatformException` propagates;
   integration tests call this directly on real hardware
5. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_result.dart` — confirm
   `isAcceptable` composition: `!blur.isBlurry && !exposure.isUnderexposed
   && !exposure.isOverexposed`; motion during capture should produce
   `blur.isBlurry == true` on real hardware
6. `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser.dart` — read
   `EventChannel` channel name; integration tests subscribe to the live
   stream and record frames during the motion window
7. `test/features/camera/sensor/stability_scorer_test.dart` — read existing
   `makeFrame` helper and test fixture patterns to reuse and extend

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define shared test fixtures and constants

Create `test/qa/stability_gate/stability_gate_fixtures.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:maya_viewer/features/camera/sensor/sensor_frame.dart';

/// Score at or below this value triggers capture rejection.
/// Matches the product-defined gate; adjust if the production threshold
/// constant is introduced before this task runs.
const double kStabilityRejectionThreshold = 0.4;

/// Score at or above this value permits capture.
const double kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold = 0.75;

/// Builds a SensorFrame representing a perfectly still device.
/// accelMag ≈ gravity (9.81 m/s²), all gyro components zero.
SensorFrame stillFrame({int timestamp = 0}) => SensorFrame(
      yaw: 0.0,
      pitch: 0.0,
      roll: 0.0,
      gyroMag: 0.0,
      accelMag: 9.81,
      timestamp: timestamp,
    );

/// Builds a SensorFrame representing vigorous hand motion.
/// gyroMag well above gyroVarianceMax threshold when variance is taken
/// over a window of these frames with alternating signs.
SensorFrame motionFrame({
  double gyroMag = 3.5,
  double accelMag = 14.0,
  double yaw = 0.8,
  double pitch = 0.6,
  double roll = 0.4,
  int timestamp = 0,
}) => SensorFrame(
      yaw: yaw,
      pitch: pitch,
      roll: roll,
      gyroMag: gyroMag,
      accelMag: accelMag,
      timestamp: timestamp,
    );

/// Builds a sequence of [count] alternating motion frames that
/// produce high gyroMag and accelMag variance in the scorer window.
/// Alternates sign on yaw/pitch/roll to maximise angular delta.
List<SensorFrame> motionSequence({
  required int count,
  int startTimestamp = 0,
  int intervalUs = 33333, // ~30 Hz
}) =>
    List.generate(count, (i) {
      final sign = i.isEven ? 1.0 : -1.0;
      return motionFrame(
        yaw: sign * 0.8,
        pitch: sign * 0.6,
        roll: sign * 0.4,
        gyroMag: 3.5,
        accelMag: 9.81 + sign * 4.0,
        timestamp: startTimestamp + i * intervalUs,
      );
    });

/// Builds a sequence of [count] still frames.
List<SensorFrame> stillSequence({
  required int count,
  int startTimestamp = 0,
  int intervalUs = 33333,
}) =>
    List.generate(
      count,
      (i) => stillFrame(timestamp: startTimestamp + i * intervalUs),
    );
```

### Step 2: Write StabilityScorer unit tests for motion rejection

Create `test/qa/stability_gate/stability_scorer_motion_test.dart`.
Use `package:test` only.

Implement ALL of the following test cases:

**group: 'StabilityScorer — motion rejection'**

1. **Full motion window → score below rejection threshold**
   Feed `windowSize` (default 30) motion frames from `motionSequence`.
   Assert `result.score < kStabilityRejectionThreshold`.

2. **Full still window → score above acceptance threshold**
   Feed `windowSize` still frames from `stillSequence`.
   Assert `result.score > kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold`.

3. **Motion frames during otherwise still window — score degrades**
   Feed 20 still frames, then 10 motion frames (total 30 = full window).
   Assert final `result.score < kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold`.
   Assert final `result.score` is strictly less than the score produced
   by a full still window.

4. **Single motion frame injected into still window does not reject**
   Feed 29 still frames then 1 motion frame.
   Assert `result.score > kStabilityRejectionThreshold`.
   (One outlier frame should not collapse the gate.)

5. **Confidence < 1.0 during window fill — score is provisional**
   Feed only 10 frames into a default scorer (windowSize=30).
   Assert `result.confidence < 1.0`.
   Assert `result.confidence ≈ 10/30` (within 1e-9).

6. **reset() followed by motion sequence rejects correctly**
   Feed `windowSize` still frames, call `reset()`, feed `windowSize`
   motion frames.
   Assert final `result.score < kStabilityRejectionThreshold`.
   (Confirms still history does not bleed into post-reset window.)

7. **Alternating still/motion frames — score in mid-range**
   Alternate still and motion frames for `windowSize` total frames.
   Assert `kStabilityRejectionThreshold < result.score
       < kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold`.

8. **Score monotonically decreases as motion frames replace still frames**
   Start with full still window (score S0). Replace oldest frames one
   by one with motion frames, recording score after each replacement
   (use a fresh scorer each iteration with N motion + (windowSize-N) still).
   Assert each successive score is ≤ the previous.

9. **High gyroMag variance alone (accel and angular delta still) → rejects**
   Build frames with `gyroMag` alternating between 0.0 and 5.0, but
   constant `accelMag: 9.81` and constant `yaw/pitch/roll: 0.0`.
   Feed `windowSize` such frames.
   Assert `result.score < kStabilityRejectionThreshold`.

10. **High angular delta alone (gyroMag and accel still) → rejects**
    Build frames with `yaw` alternating between -0.5 and +0.5 (delta >> angularDeltaMax),
    but constant `gyroMag: 0.0` and `accelMag: 9.81`.
    Feed `windowSize` such frames.
    Assert `result.score < kStabilityRejectionThreshold`.

11. **Score timestamp matches most recently added motion frame**
    Feed `motionSequence(count: windowSize, startTimestamp: 5000)`.
    Assert `result.timestamp == 5000 + (windowSize - 1) * 33333`.

### Step 3: Write gate composition unit tests

Create `test/qa/stability_gate/stability_gate_composition_test.dart`.
Use `package:test` only. Mock `FrameQualityChannel` by injecting a fake
implementation — define a local `_FakeFrameQualityChannel` that extends
or implements `FrameQualityChannel` and returns configurable results.

Define the fake at the top of the test file:

```dart
class _FakeFrameQualityChannel {
  final FrameQualityResult Function(String) _handler;
  _FakeFrameQualityChannel(this._handler);
  Future<FrameQualityResult> evaluate(String imagePath) async =>
      _handler(imagePath);
}

FrameQualityResult _blurryResult() => FrameQualityResult(
      blur: BlurResult(score: 2.1, isBlurry: true, timestamp: 1000),
      exposure: ExposureResult(
          meanLuminance: 128.0,
          isUnderexposed: false,
          isOverexposed: false,
          timestamp: 1000),
      timestamp: 1000,
    );

FrameQualityResult _sharpResult() => FrameQualityResult(
      blur: BlurResult(score: 85.0, isBlurry: false, timestamp: 1000),
      exposure: ExposureResult(
          meanLuminance: 128.0,
          isUnderexposed: false,
          isOverexposed: false,
          timestamp: 1000),
      timestamp: 1000,
    );
```

Implement ALL of the following test cases:

**group: 'Stability gate — shutter fire during motion'**

12. **Motion window + blurry frame result → isAcceptable false**
    Prime scorer with motion sequence, assert
    `stabilityScore.score < kStabilityRejectionThreshold` AND
    `fakeChannel.evaluate('/tmp/frame.jpg').isAcceptable == false`.
    Both conditions must be true simultaneously — this is the full rejection case.

13. **Still window + sharp frame result → isAcceptable true**
    Prime scorer with still sequence, assert
    `stabilityScore.score > kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold` AND
    `fakeChannel.evaluate('/tmp/frame.jpg').isAcceptable == true`.

14. **Still window + blurry frame (exposure problem) → isAcceptable false**
    Prime scorer with still sequence (high score), but fake channel returns
    `isBlurry: true`. Assert `isAcceptable == false` regardless of stability score.
    (Stability gate passing does not override blur gate.)

15. **Motion window + sharp frame (unlikely but possible) → stability rejects**
    Prime scorer with motion sequence (low score), but fake channel returns
    `isBlurry: false` (sharp frame despite motion, e.g. very fast shutter).
    Assert `stabilityScore.score < kStabilityRejectionThreshold`.
    (Stability score is independent of frame quality — document this behaviour.)

### Step 4: Build and run the integration test harness

Create `test/qa/stability_gate/stability_gate_harness.dart` as a Flutter
integration test using `package:integration_test`.

The harness performs two physical test scenarios per device:

**Scenario A — Shutter fired mid-motion (must reject)**

```dart
// 1. Subscribe to the live SensorStreamParser.frames stream.
// 2. Record frames for 2 seconds while the tester physically shakes
//    the device (prompt the tester via an on-screen instruction overlay).
// 3. At peak motion (highest gyroMag reading), trigger FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()
//    with the most recently captured frame path from CaptureController.
// 4. Assert FrameQualityResult.isAcceptable == false.
// 5. Assert StabilityScorer primed with the recorded frames produces
//    score < kStabilityRejectionThreshold.
```

**Scenario B — Shutter fired at rest (must accept)**

```dart
// 1. Subscribe to the live SensorStreamParser.frames stream.
// 2. Record frames for 3 seconds while the device rests on a flat surface
//    (prompt the tester via an on-screen instruction overlay).
// 3. Trigger FrameQualityChannel.evaluate() after the still period.
// 4. Assert FrameQualityResult.isAcceptable == true.
// 5. Assert StabilityScorer primed with the recorded frames produces
//    score > kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold.
```

Tester prompts — display these as full-screen `Text` overlays between
scenarios using a simple `ValueNotifier<String>` state; do NOT use
a Riverpod provider:
Scenario A: "SHAKE THE DEVICE VIGOROUSLY — tap screen when shaking"

Scenario B: "PLACE DEVICE FLAT AND STILL — tap screen when ready"

Collect results into a JSON record per scenario and write to the device
using `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()`:

```json
{
  "device": "<model>",
  "os": "<version>",
  "scenario": "A" | "B",
  "frame_count": int,
  "min_score": double,
  "max_score": double,
  "mean_score": double,
  "shutter_score": double,
  "shutter_timestamp": int,
  "frame_quality_acceptable": bool,
  "expected_acceptable": bool,
  "pass": bool
}
```

Assert after both scenarios:
```dart
expect(scenarioAResult['pass'], isTrue,
    reason: 'Mid-motion capture was not rejected on ${deviceModel}');
expect(scenarioBResult['pass'], isTrue,
    reason: 'Still capture was not accepted on ${deviceModel}');
```

### Step 6: Write the stability gate report

Create `test/qa/stability_gate/stability_gate_report.md` with these sections:

**1. Executive Summary**
Number of devices, scenarios run, unit tests passed, integration pass/fail
per device, overall verdict.

**2. Threshold Constants Tested**
Record `kStabilityRejectionThreshold` and `kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold`
used in this run. Record `StabilityScorer` default parameters
(`windowSize`, `gyroVarianceMax`, `accelVarianceMax`, `angularDeltaMax`)
read from source.

**3. Unit Test Results**
Table: Test case # | Description | Result (PASS/FAIL).
All 15 unit test cases must appear.

**4. Per-Device Integration Results**
For each device:
- Scenario A: shutter score, `isAcceptable`, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario B: shutter score, `isAcceptable`, PASS/FAIL
- Score distributions (min/max/mean) for both scenarios
- Verdict: PASS (both scenarios correct) / FAIL

**5. Score Separation Analysis**
Across all devices, compare mean score during motion vs mean score at rest.
State the observed separation gap. Flag if the gap is < 0.3 — this means
the threshold is dangerously close to one distribution.

**6. Threshold Adequacy**
State whether `kStabilityRejectionThreshold = 0.4` is adequate given the
observed distributions. Recommend adjustment if motion mean score > 0.35
or still mean score < 0.80.

**7. Bug Tickets**
For each integration test failure, one structured ticket:
BUG-QA-GATE-<N>
Device: <model, OS>

Severity: High

Scenario: A (mid-motion not rejected) | B (still capture not accepted)

Expected: isAcceptable = <true/false>, score <above/below> threshold

Actual: isAcceptable = <actual>, shutter_score = <value>

Steps to reproduce:

Install maya-viewer debug build <commit hash>
Run stability gate integration harness
<Shake / hold still> device as prompted
Observe shutter_score = <actual>, isAcceptable = <actual>

Proposed fix: <threshold adjustment | scorer tuning | native IMU

latency investigation>

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify `stability_scorer.dart`, `stability_score.dart`,
  `sensor_frame.dart`, `frame_quality_channel.dart`, or any production
  source file — this is an observation and validation task only
- Do NOT add `kStabilityRejectionThreshold` or `kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold`
  to production code — they are test-local constants in fixtures only; a
  separate task will introduce the production gate constant after this
  report informs the correct value
- Do NOT modify any existing unit tests in `test/features/camera/`
- Do NOT use emulators for integration test slots — IMU data on emulators
  does not reflect real motion; all integration slots must be physical hardware
- Do NOT gate the production capture flow on stability score in this task —
  the gate logic is under evaluation here, not being shipped

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Device IMU stream does not emit during the motion window (EventChannel
      silent) → harness records zero frames for scenario A; mark scenario A
      as INCONCLUSIVE (not FAIL) in the JSON and report; do not assert
- [ ] `FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()` throws `PlatformException` during
      scenario A or B → catch, record `{ "error": "<code>" }` in JSON output,
      mark scenario as INCONCLUSIVE, continue to next device; do not crash harness
- [ ] Tester shakes too gently during scenario A and motion scores stay above
      `kStabilityRejectionThreshold` → scenario A result is FAIL; file a bug
      ticket noting the device may have dampened IMU readings; do not adjust
      the threshold to compensate without wider evidence
- [ ] `StabilityScore.confidence < 1.0` at shutter-fire time (window not yet
      full when tester taps) → record confidence in JSON; note in the report
      that provisional scores were used; still assert pass/fail normally
- [ ] Both `isAcceptable` and score agree but in opposite directions
      (e.g. score above threshold but `isBlurry: true`) → record both values;
      this is a legitimate case (stable device but motion blur from subject);
      do not treat as a gate failure — document in section 5 of the report

---

## Constraints

- `kStabilityRejectionThreshold = 0.4` and `kStabilityAcceptanceThreshold = 0.75`
  are the values under evaluation in this task — do not change them mid-task;
  if the integration results suggest a different value is needed, record the
  recommendation in the report and leave the constants unchanged
- The `_FakeFrameQualityChannel` in composition tests must NOT use
  `package:mockito` or `package:mocktail` — implement as a plain Dart local
  class to keep the test file dependency-free
- Integration test scenarios A and B must be run in order (A first, B second)
  on each device — the JSON output format depends on this ordering
- Score recording in the harness must sample every frame emitted by
  `SensorStreamParser.frames` during the scenario window — do not subsample
  or debounce; the full distribution is needed for section 5 of the report
- `motionSequence` and `stillSequence` helpers in fixtures must be the
  single source of frame data for all unit tests — do not inline raw
  `SensorFrame` constructors in individual test cases

# QA: Test Focus/Exposure Lock Toggle UI
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: QA
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Validate that the focus/exposure lock toggle UI behaves correctly across states,
user interactions, and device types. The toggle controls whether the camera holds
a fixed focus distance and exposure value (locked) or continuously auto-adjusts
(unlocked). This task covers widget unit tests for the toggle component itself,
integration tests for the full lock/unlock cycle against a real camera session,
and a structured cross-device report.

- [ ] Write widget unit tests for the toggle component covering all visual states
      (unlocked, locked, transitioning, disabled) and all tap interactions
- [ ] Write unit tests for the underlying lock state model/notifier asserting
      correct state transitions and side-effect prevention when already locked
- [ ] Write integration tests on physical devices that lock focus/exposure,
      move the camera, and assert the preview does not re-adjust
- [ ] Write integration tests that unlock focus/exposure and assert
      auto-adjustment resumes
- [ ] Produce a `focus_exposure_lock_report.md` documenting widget test
      coverage, integration findings, and per-device results across 3+ devices

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/` — locate the focus/exposure lock toggle widget file
   and its controller/notifier; if the widget does not yet exist, read the
   directory structure and note the gap as an assumption — do NOT create
   production UI in this task
2. `lib/features/camera/capture_controller.dart` — read how
   `CameraController` is exposed; confirm whether `CameraController.setFocusMode()`
   and `CameraController.setExposureMode()` are already called anywhere;
   the integration tests will call these through whatever abstraction exists
3. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_channel.dart` — read
   `evaluate()` — integration tests use this to confirm exposure stability
   before and after locking
4. `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_scorer.dart` — understand that
   `StabilityScorer` is upstream of the lock toggle; confirm the lock UI
   does not interfere with the sensor pipeline
5. `test/features/camera/` — read all existing test files to understand
   naming conventions, mock patterns, and what is already covered;
   do not duplicate existing coverage
6. `pubspec.yaml` — confirm `camera` package version; `setFocusMode` and
   `setExposureMode` availability depends on version; note the version in
   the report

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Audit the production lock toggle implementation

Before writing any test, read and document the following in a file
`test/qa/focus_exposure_lock/implementation_audit.md`:

- Widget file path and class name for the toggle UI component
- State management approach (Riverpod notifier, ValueNotifier, setState, etc.)
- How `FocusMode.locked` / `FocusMode.auto` is set on `CameraController`
- How `ExposureMode.locked` / `ExposureMode.auto` is set on `CameraController`
- What visual states the toggle renders (at minimum: locked, unlocked)
- Whether focus point and exposure point are set before locking
  (`CameraController.setFocusPoint()` / `setExposurePoint()`) or if locking
  uses the current auto-selected point
- Any debounce or async guard on the toggle tap handler

If any of the above cannot be determined from source (e.g. the toggle widget
does not yet exist), document it as a gap and proceed — the unit tests
will target the `CameraController` layer directly via mocks.

### Step 2: Write toggle widget unit tests

Create `test/qa/focus_exposure_lock/focus_exposure_lock_widget_test.dart`.
Use `package:flutter_test`. If the toggle widget does not yet exist,
skip this step, document it in `implementation_audit.md`, and proceed
to Step 3.

Define a mock `CameraController` at the top of the file using a local
hand-written fake (no `mockito`) that records `setFocusMode` and
`setExposureMode` calls:

```dart
class _FakeCameraController {
  FocusMode? lastFocusMode;
  ExposureMode? lastExposureMode;
  int focusCallCount = 0;
  int exposureCallCount = 0;

  Future<void> setFocusMode(FocusMode mode) async {
    lastFocusMode = mode;
    focusCallCount++;
  }

  Future<void> setExposureMode(ExposureMode mode) async {
    lastExposureMode = mode;
    exposureCallCount++;
  }
}
```

Implement ALL of the following widget test cases:

**group: 'FocusExposureLockToggle — visual states'**

1. **Renders in unlocked state by default**
   Pump the toggle widget with initial state = unlocked.
   Assert the unlocked visual indicator is present (icon key or semantics label —
   read from production widget source; use `find.byKey` or `find.bySemanticsLabel`).
   Assert the locked indicator is absent.

2. **Renders in locked state when state = locked**
   Pump with initial state = locked.
   Assert the locked visual indicator is present.
   Assert the unlocked indicator is absent.

3. **Renders disabled state when camera is not initialized**
   Pump with `enabled: false` (or equivalent prop).
   Assert the toggle is not tappable — `find.byType(GestureDetector)` or
   `find.byType(InkWell)` has `onTap: null` or is wrapped in `IgnorePointer`.

4. **Shows transitioning/loading indicator during async lock operation**
   If the widget shows a loading state during the async `setFocusMode` call:
   Pump widget, tap toggle, do NOT await, assert loading indicator present.
   Then await, assert loading indicator absent and locked state shown.
   If the widget has no loading state: document this as a gap in the audit.

**group: 'FocusExposureLockToggle — tap interactions'**

5. **Tap on unlocked toggle calls setFocusMode(FocusMode.locked)**
   Pump unlocked toggle with fake controller injected.
   Tap toggle, await async operations.
   Assert `fake.lastFocusMode == FocusMode.locked`.
   Assert `fake.focusCallCount == 1`.

6. **Tap on unlocked toggle calls setExposureMode(ExposureMode.locked)**
   Same setup.
   Assert `fake.lastExposureMode == ExposureMode.locked`.
   Assert `fake.exposureCallCount == 1`.

7. **Tap on locked toggle calls setFocusMode(FocusMode.auto)**
   Pump locked toggle.
   Tap toggle, await.
   Assert `fake.lastFocusMode == FocusMode.auto`.

8. **Tap on locked toggle calls setExposureMode(ExposureMode.auto)**
   Same setup.
   Assert `fake.lastExposureMode == ExposureMode.auto`.

9. **Double-tap does not call setFocusMode twice in rapid succession**
   If a debounce guard exists: pump, double-tap with `pumpAndSettle`,
   assert `fake.focusCallCount == 1`.
   If no debounce: document as a gap — rapid double-tap could cause a
   lock/unlock/lock race; file BUG-QA-LOCK-DEBOUNCE in the report.

10. **Tap on disabled toggle does not call setFocusMode or setExposureMode**
    Pump with `enabled: false`, tap, assert `fake.focusCallCount == 0`.

### Step 3: Write lock state notifier unit tests

Create `test/qa/focus_exposure_lock/lock_state_notifier_test.dart`.
Use `package:test` only. Target the state management class identified
in the audit (Riverpod notifier, ChangeNotifier, or equivalent).
If no dedicated notifier exists and state lives in the widget, document
this and target the widget state via widget tests instead.

Implement ALL of the following:

**group: 'LockStateNotifier — state transitions'**

11. **Initial state is unlocked**
    Assert initial `isLocked == false`.

12. **lock() transitions to locked**
    Call `lock()`, assert `isLocked == true`.

13. **unlock() transitions to unlocked**
    Call `lock()` then `unlock()`, assert `isLocked == false`.

14. **lock() while already locked is a no-op**
    Call `lock()` twice. Assert `setFocusMode` called exactly once
    (no redundant platform call).

15. **unlock() while already unlocked is a no-op**
    Call `unlock()` twice. Assert `setExposureMode` called exactly once.

16. **lock() sets both focus and exposure atomically**
    After `lock()`, assert both `isLocked == true` and that both
    `setFocusMode(locked)` and `setExposureMode(locked)` were called
    in the same logical operation (both calls recorded before any
    state listener fires, if the notifier is observable).

17. **State is correct after lock → unlock → lock cycle**
    Call `lock()`, `unlock()`, `lock()`.
    Assert final `isLocked == true`.
    Assert `setFocusMode` called exactly twice with `FocusMode.locked`
    (once for each lock call).

### Step 4: Build the integration test harness

Create `test/qa/focus_exposure_lock/lock_integration_harness.dart`
as a Flutter integration test using `package:integration_test`.

The harness runs four scenarios in order. Display tester prompts as
full-screen `Text` overlays using a `ValueNotifier<String>` (no Riverpod):

**Scenario A — Lock then move camera (locked must not re-adjust)**
Prompt: "AIM AT A WELL-LIT SURFACE — tap to lock focus/exposure"

Steps:

Wait for CameraController to initialize and preview to render.
Record baseline ExposureResult via FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()

on the current preview frame.
Trigger the lock toggle (programmatically via the notifier, not UI tap,

to avoid flakiness from tap coordinates).
Assert CameraController.value.focusMode == FocusMode.locked.
Assert CameraController.value.exposureMode == ExposureMode.locked.
Prompt: "NOW MOVE CAMERA TO A DARK AREA — tap when done"
Record post-move ExposureResult via FrameQualityChannel.evaluate().
Assert post-move meanLuminance is within ±15.0 of baseline meanLuminance.

(Locked exposure must not re-adjust to the new lighting condition.)
Record: { scenario: 'A', baseline_luminance, post_move_luminance,

delta_luminance, focus_mode_locked: bool,

exposure_mode_locked: bool, pass: bool }


**Scenario B — Unlock after lock (auto-adjustment must resume)**
Prompt: "DEVICE SHOULD STILL BE IN DARK AREA — tap to unlock"

Steps:

Trigger unlock via notifier.
Assert CameraController.value.focusMode == FocusMode.auto.
Assert CameraController.value.exposureMode == ExposureMode.auto.
Wait 2 seconds for auto-exposure to adjust (pumpAndSettle or Future.delayed).
Record post-unlock ExposureResult via FrameQualityChannel.evaluate().
Assert post-unlock meanLuminance differs from locked baseline by > 15.0.

(Auto-exposure has re-adjusted to the dark environment.)
Record: { scenario: 'B', locked_baseline_luminance,

post_unlock_luminance, delta_luminance,

focus_mode_auto: bool, exposure_mode_auto: bool, pass: bool }


**Scenario C — Lock while device is moving (stability gate interaction)**
Prompt: "SHAKE THE DEVICE THEN TAP TO LOCK MID-MOTION"

Steps:

Subscribe to SensorStreamParser.frames, record 30 frames.
Trigger lock mid-motion.
Record StabilityScore from the window at lock time.
Assert lock completes without throwing (motion must not prevent lock call).
Assert CameraController.value.focusMode == FocusMode.locked.
Record: { scenario: 'C', stability_score_at_lock,

lock_succeeded: bool, pass: bool }

Note: pass = true if lock_succeeded == true regardless of stability score.

The stability gate and the lock toggle are independent mechanisms.


**Scenario D — Rapid lock/unlock cycle (no crash or deadlock)**
Steps (no tester prompt needed — fully automated):

Call lock(), immediately call unlock(), immediately call lock().
Await all three operations.
Assert final focusMode == FocusMode.locked.
Assert no PlatformException was thrown.
Record: { scenario: 'D', final_focus_mode, error: null | string,

pass: bool }


Assert after all scenarios:
```dart
final failures = results.where((r) => r['pass'] == false).toList();
expect(failures, isEmpty,
    reason: 'Lock toggle integration failures: ${jsonEncode(failures)}');
```

Write JSON to `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/lock_results_<deviceModel>.json`.

|------|---------------------|-------------------|
| D1   | Redmi Note 10 / 10S | Low-end Android   |

| D3   | iPhone 13 or 14     | Mid-range iOS     |

Run per Android device:
flutter drive

--target=test/qa/focus_exposure_lock/lock_integration_harness.dart 

--device-id=<DEVICE_SERIAL>

Pull results:
adb -s <SERIAL> pull 

/data/data/<app_package>/files/lock_results_<deviceModel>.json 

test/qa/focus_exposure_lock/results/

For iOS: retrieve via Xcode Devices → Download Container →
App Data → Documents.

### Step 6: Write the report

Create `test/qa/focus_exposure_lock/focus_exposure_lock_report.md`
with these sections:

**1. Executive Summary**
Devices tested, total test cases, pass/fail counts, overall verdict.

**2. Implementation Audit Summary**
Reproduce the key findings from `implementation_audit.md` in condensed form:
widget class name, state management approach, lock mechanism,
camera package version, and any gaps found.

**3. Widget Unit Test Results**
Table: Test # | Description | Result (PASS / FAIL / SKIPPED with reason).

**4. Lock State Notifier Test Results**
Table: Test # | Description | Result.

**5. Per-Device Integration Results**
For each device, a sub-section:
- Device model, OS version, test date
- Scenario A: baseline_luminance, post_move_luminance, delta, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario B: post_unlock_luminance, delta, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario C: stability_score_at_lock, lock_succeeded, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario D: final_focus_mode, error, PASS/FAIL
- Per-device overall verdict

**6. Exposure Delta Analysis**
Table: Device | Scenario A delta | Scenario B delta | A within ±15? | B > 15?
Flag any device where scenario B delta ≤ 15 — auto-exposure may not be
resuming correctly.

**7. Gaps and Observations**
List any gaps found during the audit (missing debounce, no loading state, etc.)
and any unexpected behaviours observed during integration runs
(e.g. lock silently failing on a specific device, race condition in rapid cycle).

**8. Bug Tickets**
One structured ticket per failure:
BUG-QA-LOCK-<N>
Device: <model, OS> | Severity: High / Medium

Summary: <one-line description>

Scenario: <A | B | C | D | Widget test #N>

Expected: <what should happen>

Actual: <what happened, include values>

Steps to reproduce:


<specific steps>


Proposed fix: <specific code location and change>

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify the toggle widget, lock notifier, or any production source
  file — observation and validation only; production changes require a
  separate task informed by this report
- Do NOT modify existing tests in `test/features/camera/`
- Do NOT add `kStabilityRejectionThreshold` or any production threshold
  constant to production code in this task
- Do NOT use Riverpod providers in the integration harness — use
  `ValueNotifier` for tester prompts and direct class instantiation for
  notifier testing
- Do NOT use `mockito` or `mocktail` — all fakes are hand-written local
  classes as established in prior QA tasks

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `setFocusMode()` throws `PlatformException` on a device that does not
      support focus locking (some low-end Android devices) → catch in the
      integration harness, mark scenario A as INCONCLUSIVE (not FAIL),
      record `{ "error": "FOCUS_LOCK_UNSUPPORTED" }` in JSON, document in report
- [ ] `setExposureMode()` throws separately from `setFocusMode()` (iOS can
      lock them independently; Android locks them together) → catch each
      independently; if one succeeds and one fails, record partial lock state
      and flag in section 7 of the report
- [ ] Scenario B delta ≤ 15 despite unlocking (auto-exposure too slow or
      lighting conditions did not change enough) → mark FAIL; instruct the
      tester in the report to ensure the dark area is significantly darker
      than the well-lit surface (at least 3 stops difference)
- [ ] Scenario D rapid cycle causes a deadlock (future never resolves on
      a specific device) → apply a 5-second timeout via `Future.timeout()`;
      if timeout fires, mark FAIL and record `{ "error": "TIMEOUT" }`
- [ ] Toggle widget does not exist yet (identified in audit) → skip all
      widget tests (Steps 2 and state notifier tests targeting widget state),
      mark them SKIPPED in the report with reason "Widget not yet implemented",
      proceed with integration tests targeting `CameraController` directly

---

## Constraints

- The ±15.0 `meanLuminance` threshold for scenario A and the >15.0 threshold
  for scenario B are QA constants local to the harness — do NOT add them to
  production code; define them as constants at the top of the harness file:
  `const double kLockStabilityDelta = 15.0;`
- `_FakeCameraController` must record calls synchronously before returning
  the future — this ensures call-count assertions are not subject to async
  timing in tests
- Scenario C must NOT assert `isAcceptable == false` — the stability gate
  and lock toggle are independent; locking mid-motion is a valid user
  action and must not be blocked by stability score
- The `implementation_audit.md` must be written before any test code is
  created — the audit findings determine which test cases can run and
  which must be marked SKIPPED
- All four integration scenarios must run in a single harness invocation
  per device — do not split into multiple `flutter drive` commands

---

      prevent locking)

  and B; if this is not achievable in the test environment, cover a portion
  of the lens between scenarios as an alternative — document in the report

  `CameraController.value.exposureMode` are readable after calling the
  setter methods — this is true for `camera` package ≥ 0.10.0; if the

  fake controller call records as the only verification mechanism

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Test camera preview survives app backgrounding (especially iOS) — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>>>>



Here is the agent-ready coding prompt:

# QA: Test Camera Preview Survives App Backgrounding (especially iOS)
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: QA
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Validate that the camera preview correctly suspends when the app is backgrounded
and fully recovers when the app returns to the foreground — without crash, black
screen, frozen frame, resource leak, or duplicate CameraController initialisation.
iOS is the primary risk platform due to AVCaptureSession lifecycle requirements;
Android is a required regression target. This task covers lifecycle unit tests,
widget tests for the preview widget's lifecycle hooks, integration tests using
real backgrounding on physical devices, and a structured cross-device report.

- [ ] Write unit tests for the `CaptureController` lifecycle methods
      (`didChangeAppLifecycleState`) asserting correct dispose/reinitialise
      behaviour on background and foreground transitions
- [ ] Write widget tests asserting the camera preview widget responds
      correctly to `AppLifecycleState` changes without accessing a disposed
      `CameraController`
- [ ] Write integration tests on physical devices that background and
      foreground the app and assert the preview recovers to a live feed
      within an acceptable time window
- [ ] Test the double-background edge case (background → background again
      before foreground) and the rapid cycle edge case (background →
      foreground within 1 second)
- [ ] Produce a `backgrounding_report.md` documenting lifecycle findings,
      timing measurements, and per-device results across 3+ devices with
      iOS as a mandatory slot

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/capture_controller.dart` — THIS IS THE PRIMARY FILE.
   Read the full `CaptureController` implementation; confirm whether it
   implements `WidgetsBindingObserver` and `didChangeAppLifecycleState`;
   if it does not, document this as a critical gap in the audit — iOS
   will crash or freeze without it; read how `CameraController` is
   initialised and disposed; note whether `dispose()` is called on
   background and whether reinitialisation is triggered on foreground
2. `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser.dart` — confirm the
   `EventChannel` subscription is also torn down or left running on
   background; if it keeps running while the camera is disposed, document
   as a gap
3. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_channel.dart` — confirm
   whether `evaluate()` can be safely called during a transition; it must
   not be called while `CameraController` is disposed
4. `lib/main.dart` or `lib/app.dart` — confirm `ProviderScope` placement
   and whether `CaptureController` is disposed when the widget tree is torn
   down vs surviving as a root-scoped provider
5. `ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift` — read for any existing
   `applicationWillResignActive` / `applicationDidBecomeActive` hooks that
   may interact with the camera session; do NOT modify
6. `android/app/src/main/kotlin/` — read for any existing
   `onPause` / `onResume` Activity hooks; do NOT modify
7. `test/features/camera/` — read all existing tests to avoid duplication
   and understand the `_FakeCameraController` pattern established in
   prior QA tasks

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Audit the lifecycle implementation

Before writing any test, create
`test/qa/backgrounding/lifecycle_audit.md` documenting:

- Does `CaptureController` implement `WidgetsBindingObserver`? (yes / no / partial)
- Is `WidgetsBinding.instance.addObserver(this)` called in `build()` or
  `init()`? Is `removeObserver` called in `dispose()`?
- What happens in `didChangeAppLifecycleState` for each state:
  `inactive`, `paused`, `resumed`, `detached`, `hidden` (Flutter 3.13+)?
- Is `_cameraController.dispose()` called on `paused`?
- Is a new `CameraController` initialised on `resumed`?
- Is there a guard preventing double-initialisation if `resumed` fires
  while a previous init is still in progress?
- Does the `EventChannel` subscription in `SensorStreamParser` survive
  backgrounding or get torn down?
- iOS-specific: is `AVCaptureSession.stopRunning()` called via the
  `CameraController.dispose()` path, or is there a separate native hook?
- Any known crash logs or Sentry events related to backgrounding?

If `WidgetsBindingObserver` is NOT implemented, flag this as
CRITICAL GAP — every subsequent test will document the failure mode,
but the fix must be tracked as BUG-QA-BG-OBSERVER in the report.

### Step 2: Write CaptureController lifecycle unit tests

Create `test/qa/backgrounding/capture_controller_lifecycle_test.dart`.
Use `package:flutter_test`.

Define a lifecycle-aware fake at the top of the file. Because
`CaptureController` is an `AsyncNotifier` and interacts with
`WidgetsBinding`, use `TestWidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized()`
and simulate lifecycle state changes via:

```dart
void _sendLifecycleEvent(AppLifecycleState state) {
  final binding = TestWidgetsFlutterBinding.instance;
  binding.handleAppLifecycleStateChanged(state);
}
```

Define a call-recording fake:

```dart
class _FakeCameraController {
  int initializeCallCount = 0;
  int disposeCallCount = 0;
  bool isInitialized = false;

  Future<void> initialize() async {
    initializeCallCount++;
    isInitialized = true;
  }

  Future<void> dispose() async {
    disposeCallCount++;
    isInitialized = false;
  }

  CameraValue get value => isInitialized
      ? const CameraValue.uninitialized().copyWith(isInitialized: true)
      : const CameraValue.uninitialized();
}
```

Implement ALL of the following test cases:

**group: 'CaptureController — lifecycle transitions'**

1. **background (paused) → dispose() called exactly once**
   Initialize controller, send `AppLifecycleState.paused`.
   Assert `fake.disposeCallCount == 1`.

2. **foreground (resumed) → initialize() called after previous dispose**
   Send `paused` then `resumed`.
   Assert `fake.initializeCallCount == 2` (once at startup, once on resume).
   Assert `fake.isInitialized == true`.

3. **inactive → does NOT dispose (iOS intermediate state)**
   Send `AppLifecycleState.inactive`.
   Assert `fake.disposeCallCount == 0`.
   (iOS sends `inactive` before `paused`; disposing on `inactive` causes
   a double-dispose when `paused` arrives milliseconds later.)

4. **paused → resumed → paused: dispose and reinitialise cycle is correct**
   Send: `paused`, `resumed`, `paused`.
   Assert `fake.disposeCallCount == 2`.
   Assert `fake.initializeCallCount == 2` (startup + first resume).
   Assert `fake.isInitialized == false` (ended in paused state).

5. **rapid resumed during in-progress initialisation does not double-init**
   Simulate: send `paused`, then send `resumed` twice before the first
   `initialize()` future completes (use a `Completer` in the fake to
   control timing).
   Assert `fake.initializeCallCount == 2` total (startup + exactly one
   resume init), not 3.

6. **detached → dispose() called (app killed)**
   Send `AppLifecycleState.detached`.
   Assert `fake.disposeCallCount == 1`.

7. **hidden (Flutter 3.13+) → same behaviour as paused**
   If `AppLifecycleState.hidden` exists in the Flutter version used:
   Send `AppLifecycleState.hidden`.
   Assert `fake.disposeCallCount == 1`.
   If `hidden` does not exist in the current Flutter version, mark
   this test SKIPPED and document the Flutter version in the audit.

8. **observer registered on init, removed on dispose**
   Assert `WidgetsBinding.instance.observers` contains the
   `CaptureController` instance after initialisation.
   Call controller `dispose()`.
   Assert it is no longer in `observers`.
   (Leaked observer causes memory leak and ghost lifecycle callbacks
   on subsequent screens.)

9. **no crash when paused fires before first initialization completes**
   Simulate the race: begin `initialize()` with a delayed fake, send
   `paused` before the future resolves.
   Assert no exception is thrown and `fake.disposeCallCount == 0`
   (cannot dispose what was never initialized).

### Step 3: Write camera preview widget lifecycle tests

Create `test/qa/backgrounding/camera_preview_lifecycle_widget_test.dart`.
Use `package:flutter_test`.

Implement ALL of the following widget test cases:

**group: 'CameraPreview widget — lifecycle'**

10. **Preview renders CameraPreview when controller is initialized**
    Pump the preview widget with a fully initialized fake controller.
    Assert `find.byType(CameraPreview)` is present.

11. **Preview renders loading placeholder when controller is loading
    (AsyncValue.loading)**
    Pump with `AsyncValue.loading()`.
    Assert `find.byType(CameraLoadingPlaceholder)` is present.
    Assert `find.byType(CameraPreview)` is absent.

12. **Preview renders error placeholder after background dispose**
    Pump with initialized controller, then simulate `paused` which
    disposes the controller and sets state to `AsyncValue.loading()`
    or `AsyncValue.error()`.
    Assert `CameraPreview` is no longer present.
    Assert placeholder (loading or error) is present.
    (No black screen — user must see something during reinitialisation.)

13. **Preview recovers to CameraPreview after foreground reinitialise**
    From the state in test #12, simulate `resumed` and await
    reinitialisation.
    Assert `find.byType(CameraPreview)` is present again.

14. **No setState called on disposed widget during background transition**
    Pump preview widget, pump a frame, then remove it from the tree
    (`pumpWidget(const SizedBox.shrink())`), then send `paused`.
    Assert no `setState called after dispose` Flutter error is thrown.
    (This is the most common iOS crash pattern — lifecycle callback fires
    after the widget is already gone.)

15. **CameraPreview is not constructed with a disposed CameraController**
    After backgrounding, assert that `CameraPreview` is not present in
    the tree while `fake.isInitialized == false`.
    (Passing a disposed controller to `CameraPreview` causes a black
    screen on iOS and a platform exception on Android.)

### Step 4: Build the integration test harness

Create `test/qa/backgrounding/backgrounding_harness.dart` as a Flutter
integration test using `package:integration_test`.

The harness requires the tester to manually background and foreground the
app via the device's home button / app switcher. Display prompts as
full-screen `Text` overlays using `ValueNotifier<String>`.

The harness runs five scenarios in order:

**Scenario A — Standard background/foreground cycle**
Prompt: "TAP HOME BUTTON — then return to this app within 5 seconds"

Steps:

Confirm preview is live: record PreviewState.live.
Start a timer when prompt is shown.
Poll CaptureController state every 500ms for up to 10 seconds after

the tester taps to confirm they have returned.
Assert CaptureController state transitions through:

loading/disposed → initializing → live (in that order).
Measure time from app-foreground signal to first live preview frame:

record as recovery_time_ms.
Assert recovery_time_ms < 3000 (3 second budget).
Record: { scenario: 'A', recovery_time_ms, transitioned_correctly: bool,

pass: bool }


**Scenario B — Extended background (30 seconds)**
Prompt: "TAP HOME BUTTON — wait 30 seconds — then return"

Steps:

Confirm preview is live before backgrounding.
After tester returns, assert preview recovers within 5 seconds.

(Extended background may cause iOS to fully tear down AVCaptureSession.)
Record: { scenario: 'B', recovery_time_ms, pass: bool }

pass = recovery_time_ms < 5000


**Scenario C — Rapid background/foreground (< 1 second)**
Prompt: "TAP HOME BUTTON THEN IMMEDIATELY RETURN (as fast as possible)"

Steps:

After tester returns, await up to 5 seconds for preview to be live.
Assert no crash occurred (harness is still running).
Assert CaptureController is not in a permanently broken state

(not stuck in loading/error after 5 seconds).
Record: { scenario: 'C', recovery_time_ms,

stuck_in_error: bool, pass: bool }


**Scenario D — Background during active capture (triggerCapture in flight)**
Prompt: "TAP TO START CAPTURE — then IMMEDIATELY TAP HOME BUTTON"

Steps:

Call CaptureController.triggerCapture() — do NOT await.
Immediately simulate or prompt background (within ~200ms).
Foreground after 3 seconds.
Assert no PlatformException from the interrupted capture propagates

to the UI as an unhandled error.
Assert preview recovers correctly.
Record: { scenario: 'D', capture_interrupted: bool,

unhandled_error: bool, preview_recovered: bool, pass: bool }

pass = !unhandled_error && preview_recovered


**Scenario E — Double background (background without foregrounding first)**
Note: This scenario tests the observer deduplication guard.

Steps (automated — no tester prompt):

Send AppLifecycleState.paused programmatically via

ServicesBinding.instance.handlePlatformMessage or equivalent.
Send AppLifecycleState.paused again immediately.
Assert disposeCallCount == 1 (not 2).
Send AppLifecycleState.resumed.
Assert initializeCallCount incremented by exactly 1.
Record: { scenario: 'E', double_dispose_occurred: bool, pass: bool }

pass = !double_dispose_occurred


Measure `recovery_time_ms` by recording `DateTime.now()` when the foreground
lifecycle event fires and when `CaptureController` state first becomes
`AsyncData<CameraController>` with `isInitialized == true`.

Write JSON to `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/bg_results_<deviceModel>.json`.

Assert after all scenarios:
```dart
final failures = results.where((r) => r['pass'] == false).toList();
expect(failures, isEmpty,
    reason: 'Backgrounding failures: ${jsonEncode(failures)}');
```

### Step 5: Execute on 3+ physical devices

Minimum device spread — iOS is mandatory:

| Slot | Target device       | Category             | Priority   |
|------|---------------------|----------------------|------------|
| D1   | iPhone 13 or 14     | Mid-range iOS        | MANDATORY  |
| D2   | Redmi Note 10 / 10S | Low-end Android      | Required   |
| D3   | Samsung Galaxy S23  | Flagship Android     | Required   |
| D4   | iPhone SE 3         | Low-end iOS (optional)| Recommended|

Run per Android device:
flutter drive 

--driver=test_driver/integration_test.dart 

--target=test/qa/backgrounding/backgrounding_harness.dart 

--device-id=<DEVICE_SERIAL>

For iOS, run via Xcode:
flutter drive 

--driver=test_driver/integration_test.dart 

--target=test/qa/backgrounding/backgrounding_harness.dart 

--device-id=<IPHONE_UDID>

Pull Android results:
adb -s <SERIAL> pull 

/data/data/<app_package>/files/bg_results_<deviceModel>.json 

test/qa/backgrounding/results/

Pull iOS results via Xcode Devices → Download Container →
App Data → Documents.

### Step 6: Write the report

Create `test/qa/backgrounding/backgrounding_report.md` with these sections:

**1. Executive Summary**
Devices tested, total test cases (unit + widget + integration), pass/fail
summary, iOS-specific findings, overall verdict.

**2. Lifecycle Implementation Audit**
Reproduce key findings from `lifecycle_audit.md` in condensed form.
Flag CRITICAL GAP if `WidgetsBindingObserver` is not implemented.

**3. Unit Test Results**
Table: Test # | Description | Result (PASS / FAIL / SKIPPED + reason).

**4. Widget Test Results**
Table: Test # | Description | Result.

**5. Per-Device Integration Results**
For each device, a sub-section:
- Scenario A: recovery_time_ms, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario B: recovery_time_ms, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario C: recovery_time_ms, stuck_in_error, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario D: capture_interrupted, unhandled_error, preview_recovered, PASS/FAIL
- Scenario E: double_dispose_occurred, PASS/FAIL
- Per-device overall verdict

**6. Recovery Time Analysis**
Table: Device | Scenario A ms | Scenario B ms | Scenario C ms |
       Within budget? (A<3000, B<5000)
Flag any device exceeding the time budgets.

**7. iOS-Specific Findings**
Dedicated section for iOS observations:
- Did AVCaptureSession tear down correctly on background?
- Was there a black screen period on foreground?
- Did the `inactive` → `paused` sequence cause any double-dispose?
- Any differences between iPhone SE (lower-end) and iPhone 13/14?

**8. Gaps and Risk Areas**
List gaps from the audit. List any unexpected behaviours.
Call out specifically: observer leak risk, double-init risk,
black screen risk, and interrupted capture risk.

**9. Bug Tickets**
One structured ticket per failure:
BUG-QA-BG-<N>
Device: <model, OS> | Severity: Critical / High / Medium

Summary: <one-line description>

Scenario / Test: <A–E | unit test #N | widget test #N>

Expected: <behaviour>

Actual: <behaviour with values>

Steps to reproduce:


<specific reproducible steps>


Proposed fix: <file path, method name, specific change>

Severity guide:
- Critical: crash, unhandled exception, or permanent black screen
- High: recovery > 5 seconds or double-dispose/double-init
- Medium: recovery > 3 seconds (scenario A budget), cosmetic flicker

### Step 7: Commit outputs

Commit to the branch:
- `test/qa/backgrounding/lifecycle_audit.md`
- `test/qa/backgrounding/capture_controller_lifecycle_test.dart`
- `test/qa/backgrounding/camera_preview_lifecycle_widget_test.dart`
- `test/qa/backgrounding/backgrounding_harness.dart`
- `test/qa/backgrounding/results/bg_results_<deviceModel>.json` × N
- `test/qa/backgrounding/backgrounding_report.md`

Do NOT commit changes to any file outside `test/qa/backgrounding/`.

---

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify `capture_controller.dart`, `sensor_stream_parser.dart`,
  or any production source file — observation and documentation only;
  lifecycle fixes require a separate task informed by this report
- Do NOT modify existing tests in `test/features/camera/`
- Do NOT add `WidgetsBindingObserver` to `CaptureController` in this task
  even if the audit reveals it is missing — document the gap, file the
  bug ticket, and leave the fix to a dedicated implementation task
- Do NOT use `mockito` or `mocktail` — all fakes are hand-written local
  classes per the project convention established in prior QA tasks
- Do NOT trigger backgrounding programmatically via private Flutter
  internals in integration tests for scenarios A–D — the tester must
  use the real home button / app switcher to exercise the true iOS
  AVCaptureSession teardown path; scenario E (double-background) is
  the only scenario where programmatic lifecycle injection is acceptable

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `CaptureController` state is `AsyncLoading` when `paused` fires
      (initialisation was in progress) → dispose must be a no-op if
      `CameraController` was never fully initialised; unit test #9 covers
      this; integration harness must handle gracefully without crash
- [ ] `resumed` fires multiple times without intervening `paused`
      (observed on some Android OEM skins) → reinitialisation guard
      in unit test #5; integration scenario E covers the double-resume
      analogue; if observed in integration results, document in section 8
- [ ] iOS `inactive` state fires on incoming call, FaceID prompt,
      notification pull-down — must NOT dispose camera; unit test #3
      is the guard; if test #3 fails, this is a HIGH severity issue
      for any AR feature that requires continuous preview
- [ ] Camera permission revoked while app is backgrounded (iOS Settings
      → Privacy → Camera → toggle off) → on foreground, initialisation
      throws `CameraException` with code `cameraPermission`; harness
      must not test this scenario actively but must not crash if it
      occurs; catch `CameraException` in the harness and record
      `{ "error": "PERMISSION_REVOKED" }` as INCONCLUSIVE
- [ ] Second `backgrounding_harness.dart` run on same device without
      reinstall → JSON output file already exists; overwrite it;
      use `File.writeAsString()` without append mode
- [ ] Integration test harness loses sync with the tester (tester takes
      > 10 seconds for scenario A prompt) → apply per-scenario timeouts:
      scenario A: 15 seconds, B: 45 seconds, C: 8 seconds; if timeout
      fires, record `{ "error": "TESTER_TIMEOUT" }` as INCONCLUSIVE

---

## Constraints

- Recovery time budgets are QA constants local to the harness — define at
  the top of `backgrounding_harness.dart`:
```dart
  const int kScenarioARecoveryBudgetMs = 3000;
  const int kScenarioBRecoveryBudgetMs = 5000;
  const int kScenarioCRecoveryBudgetMs = 5000;
```
  Do NOT add these to production code
- iOS is a mandatory device slot — this task is specifically motivated by
  iOS AVCaptureSession behaviour; running only Android devices is not
  sufficient to mark the task complete
- Unit tests must use `TestWidgetsFlutterBinding` to simulate lifecycle
  events — do not use `WidgetsBinding.instance` directly in tests as it
  may reference the production binding
- The `lifecycle_audit.md` must be completed before any test is written —
  if `WidgetsBindingObserver` is absent, every lifecycle unit test is
  expected to FAIL; document this upfront rather than investigating per-test
- Scenario D (capture during background) must use a real
  `triggerCapture()` call, not a mocked one — the race condition between
  capture and backgrounding is a native-layer concern that mocks cannot
  reproduce

  in the CaptureController task; if it uses `StateNotifier` or `ChangeNotifier`,
  the lifecycle unit test setup must be adjusted — document in the audit

  exist and are rendered by the preview widget during non-live states;
  if they do not exist, widget tests #11, #12, #13 are SKIPPED with
  reason "Placeholder widgets not yet implemented"

  `CaptureController` — if one exists, some unit tests may pass trivially;
  document the finding in the audit and note which tests were informative
  vs confirmatory
- Assumed: `triggerCapture()` in scenario D can be interrupted mid-flight
  without data corruption — if the native capture pipeline has a mutex that
  blocks dispose until capture completes, scenario D may take longer than
  expected; record the actual timing in the JSON and flag in section 8

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Test folder creation and cleanup across project lifecycle — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>>


Here is the agent-ready coding prompt:

# QA: Test Folder Creation and Cleanup Across Project Lifecycle
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: QA
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Validate that the app's file system folder structure is created correctly
at the right lifecycle moment, persists across the full project session,
and is cleaned up completely when the project ends or the app is uninstalled.
Orphaned folders, missing directories at capture time, and leftover files
after cleanup are all defects that corrupt the user's storage and break
downstream pipeline steps (blur detection, frame quality analysis, export).
This task covers unit tests for the folder management logic, integration
tests across the full project lifecycle on physical devices, and a
cross-platform report with iOS and Android coverage.

- [ ] Audit and document the folder creation and cleanup strategy currently
      implemented in the codebase
- [ ] Write unit tests for the folder manager class covering creation,
      idempotency, cleanup, partial cleanup failure, and path correctness
- [ ] Write integration tests on physical devices exercising the full
      lifecycle: app launch → project start → capture → project end →
      folder verification
- [ ] Test cleanup under abnormal termination (app killed mid-session,
      backgrounding during cleanup, storage full simulation)
- [ ] Produce a `folder_lifecycle_report.md` documenting findings,
      orphan risks, and per-device results across 3+ devices

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/` — search for any folder/directory management
   class or utility (e.g. `CameraFolderManager`, `ProjectStorage`,
   `CapturePaths`, or similar); if none exists, document as CRITICAL GAP
   in the audit — captured frames from `CaptureController.triggerCapture()`
   have nowhere to land
2. `lib/features/camera/capture_controller.dart` — read where captured
   frame paths are constructed; confirm what base directory is used
   (app documents, app cache, external storage); confirm whether the
   directory is created before `takePicture()` is called or assumed
   to exist
3. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_channel.dart` — read
   `evaluate(String imagePath)`; this method will fail silently or throw
   `FILE_NOT_FOUND` if the folder was not created before capture; confirm
   the dependency chain
4. `pubspec.yaml` — confirm `path_provider` version; note whether
   `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()`, `getApplicationCacheDirectory()`,
   and `getTemporaryDirectory()` are all used or just one; different
   directories have different OS cleanup behaviour (cache vs documents)
5. `android/app/src/main/kotlin/` — search for any native folder creation
   or `File.mkdirs()` calls; confirm whether folder creation is handled
   natively or purely in Dart
6. `ios/Runner/` — search for any native `FileManager.createDirectory`
   calls; same question as Android
7. `test/features/camera/` — read all existing tests to avoid duplication
   and understand established fake/mock patterns

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Audit the folder management implementation

Before writing any test, create
`test/qa/folder_lifecycle/folder_audit.md` documenting:

- **Folder structure**: list every directory the app creates under the
  app's storage root (e.g. `<documents>/projects/<id>/frames/`,
  `<documents>/projects/<id>/exports/`, `<cache>/qa_images/`, etc.)
  — use exact relative paths
- **Creation trigger**: when is each directory created? (app launch,
  project start, first capture, on-demand)
- **Creation method**: which Dart or native API creates the directory?
  (`Directory.create(recursive: true)`, native `mkdirs()`, etc.)
- **Cleanup trigger**: when is each directory deleted? (project end,
  app uninstall, manual user action, never)
- **Cleanup method**: `Directory.delete(recursive: true)`, file-by-file,
  native hook, or missing entirely
- **Base path**: which `path_provider` directory is used for each folder
  type? (documents = persists across app updates; cache = OS may purge;
  temp = OS purges freely)
- **Project ID**: how is a project ID generated and used in paths?
  (UUID, timestamp, sequential integer) — a collision in project IDs
  means folder deletion deletes the wrong project's data
- **Concurrent project risk**: can two projects share a folder? What
  happens if `delete(recursive: true)` is called while a second project
  is writing to the same root?
- Any known issues with folder creation on Android scoped storage
  (API 29+) or iOS Files app visibility

If no folder management class exists, every item above is MISSING —
document each as such and proceed; the integration tests will document
the resulting failures (e.g. `FILE_NOT_FOUND` from
`FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()`).

### Step 2: Write folder manager unit tests

Create `test/qa/folder_lifecycle/folder_manager_unit_test.dart`.
Use `package:test` only.

If no folder manager class exists, create a minimal test-local stub
to document the expected interface — mark all tests FAILING with reason
"FolderManager not yet implemented", then proceed to integration tests.

If the class exists, use `Directory.systemTemp` as the base path in all
unit tests — never write to `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()` in unit
tests (requires a real Flutter binding). Inject the base path via
constructor or factory parameter.

Define a test helper at the top of the file:

```dart
import 'dart:io';
import 'package:test/test.dart';

/// Creates a fresh temp directory for each test and cleans it up after.
Directory useTempDir() {
  late Directory dir;
  setUp(() {
    dir = Directory.systemTemp.createTempSync('folder_manager_test_');
  });
  tearDown(() {
    if (dir.existsSync()) dir.deleteSync(recursive: true);
  });
  return dir; // caller assigns in test body via late binding
}
```

Implement ALL of the following unit test cases:

**group: 'FolderManager — creation'**

1. **createProjectFolder() creates the expected directory structure**
   Call `createProjectFolder(projectId: 'test-001', baseDir: tempDir)`.
   Assert `<tempDir>/projects/test-001/frames/` exists.
   Assert `<tempDir>/projects/test-001/exports/` exists.
   (Adjust subdirectory names to match what the audit found.)

2. **createProjectFolder() is idempotent — calling twice does not throw**
   Call `createProjectFolder()` twice with the same project ID.
   Assert no exception is thrown.
   Assert directory still exists and contains expected subdirectories.

3. **createProjectFolder() creates intermediate directories (recursive)**
   Use a base path with a non-existent intermediate directory.
   Assert creation succeeds without requiring the caller to pre-create
   the parent.

4. **Correct absolute path returned for frames directory**
   Assert the returned frames path ends with
   `/projects/test-001/frames` (or the equivalent identified in audit).
   Assert it is an absolute path (starts with `/`).

5. **Correct absolute path returned for exports directory**
   Same pattern for exports subdirectory.

6. **createProjectFolder() with empty projectId throws ArgumentError**
   Assert `createProjectFolder(projectId: '', baseDir: tempDir)` throws
   `ArgumentError` with message containing `'projectId'`.

7. **createProjectFolder() with projectId containing path separators throws**
   Assert `createProjectFolder(projectId: '../escape', baseDir: tempDir)`
   throws `ArgumentError`.
   (Path traversal guard — a malicious or malformed project ID must not
   write outside the projects directory.)

**group: 'FolderManager — cleanup'**

8. **deleteProjectFolder() removes the entire project directory tree**
   Create a project folder, write a dummy file to `frames/`.
   Call `deleteProjectFolder(projectId: 'test-001', baseDir: tempDir)`.
   Assert `<tempDir>/projects/test-001/` does not exist.

9. **deleteProjectFolder() is a no-op if directory does not exist**
   Call `deleteProjectFolder()` on a project ID that was never created.
   Assert no exception is thrown.

10. **deleteProjectFolder() does not affect sibling project directories**
    Create `test-001` and `test-002` project folders.
    Delete `test-001`.
    Assert `<tempDir>/projects/test-002/` still exists.

11. **deleteAllProjectFolders() removes all projects under base dir**
    Create `test-001`, `test-002`, `test-003`.
    Call `deleteAllProjectFolders(baseDir: tempDir)`.
    Assert `<tempDir>/projects/` is empty or does not exist.

12. **deleteAllProjectFolders() is a no-op when projects dir is empty**
    Assert no exception when called on a base dir with no projects.

13. **Partial write (incomplete capture) — cleanup still succeeds**
    Create project folder, write a 0-byte file (simulating interrupted
    capture) to `frames/frame_001.jpg`.
    Assert `deleteProjectFolder()` succeeds and removes the 0-byte file.

14. **listProjectFolders() returns all created project IDs**
    Create `test-001`, `test-002`.
    Assert `listProjectFolders(baseDir: tempDir)` returns a list
    containing exactly `['test-001', 'test-002']` (order not asserted).

15. **listProjectFolders() returns empty list when none exist**
    Assert returns `[]`, not null or exception.

**group: 'FolderManager — path safety'**

16. **framePathFor() returns path inside the frames subdirectory**
    Assert `framePathFor(projectId: 'test-001', frameIndex: 3, baseDir: tempDir)`
    returns a path matching `*/projects/test-001/frames/frame_003.jpg`
    (or the naming convention identified in audit).

17. **framePathFor() with negative frameIndex throws ArgumentError**
    Assert throws `ArgumentError`.

18. **Two projects with different IDs produce non-overlapping frame paths**
    Assert `framePathFor(projectId: 'A', ...)` and
    `framePathFor(projectId: 'B', ...)` share no common prefix beyond
    the base directory.

### Step 3: Build the integration test harness

Create `test/qa/folder_lifecycle/folder_lifecycle_harness.dart`
as a Flutter integration test using `package:integration_test`.

Display tester prompts using `ValueNotifier<String>` full-screen overlays.
No Riverpod in the harness.

The harness runs six scenarios in order:

**Scenario A — Full project lifecycle (create → capture → cleanup)**
Steps (automated):

Obtain base directory via path_provider.
Call FolderManager.createProjectFolder(projectId: 'qa-lifecycle-A').
Assert the frames and exports subdirectories exist on disk.
Call CaptureController.triggerCapture() — await completion.
Assert the captured JPEG file exists at the expected frame path.
Assert file size > 0 bytes.
Call FrameQualityChannel.evaluate(framePath) — assert no FILE_NOT_FOUND.
Call FolderManager.deleteProjectFolder(projectId: 'qa-lifecycle-A').
Assert the project directory no longer exists.
Record: { scenario: 'A', folder_created: bool, frame_written: bool,

file_size_bytes: int, quality_eval_succeeded: bool,

folder_deleted: bool, pass: bool }


**Scenario B — App kill during capture (orphan folder risk)**
Prompt: "TAP TO START CAPTURE — then FORCE-KILL AND RELAUNCH THE APP

via the app switcher before the capture completes"

Steps after relaunch:

List all project folders under the base directory.
Assert any orphaned 'qa-lifecycle-B' folder is detectable via

FolderManager.listProjectFolders().
Assert cleanup of orphaned folder succeeds:

FolderManager.deleteProjectFolder(projectId: 'qa-lifecycle-B').
Assert no orphaned folder remains.
Record: { scenario: 'B', orphan_detected: bool,

orphan_cleaned: bool, pass: bool }

pass = orphan_cleaned == true


**Scenario C — Cleanup during active background**
Prompt: "TAP TO START CLEANUP — then IMMEDIATELY TAP HOME BUTTON"

Steps:

Create 'qa-lifecycle-C' project folder with dummy frames.
Begin deleteProjectFolder() — do NOT await.
Tester backgrounds the app mid-deletion.
Tester returns within 5 seconds.
Assert project folder is either fully deleted or fully intact

(no partial state — no empty subdirectories with no parent).
If partially deleted, call deleteProjectFolder() again.
Assert final state: folder does not exist.
Record: { scenario: 'C', partial_state_detected: bool,

recovery_cleanup_needed: bool, pass: bool }

pass = final folder does not exist


**Scenario D — Multiple projects coexist without interference**
Steps (automated):

Create 'qa-d-001', 'qa-d-002', 'qa-d-003' simultaneously.
Write a unique dummy file to each project's frames dir.
Delete 'qa-d-002' only.
Assert 'qa-d-001' and 'qa-d-003' and their files still exist.
Assert 'qa-d-002' is gone.
Cleanup 'qa-d-001' and 'qa-d-003'.
Record: { scenario: 'D', sibling_contamination: bool, pass: bool }

pass = !sibling_contamination


**Scenario E — Storage pressure (low-storage simulation)**
Steps (automated — no tester prompt):

Fill a temp subdirectory with dummy data until < 50 MB free

(use dart:io Directory and File to write a large dummy file;

skip this scenario and mark SKIPPED if device has > 5 GB free

and filling would take > 30 seconds).
Attempt FolderManager.createProjectFolder(projectId: 'qa-e-001').
Assert either:

a. Folder is created successfully (sufficient space existed), OR

b. A descriptive exception is thrown (not an unhandled null pointer).
Cleanup the dummy fill file.
Record: { scenario: 'E', creation_succeeded: bool,

error_type: null | string, pass: bool }

pass = no unhandled exception (either outcome a or b is acceptable)


**Scenario F — Path traversal guard on device**
Steps (automated):

Attempt FolderManager.createProjectFolder(projectId: '../escape').
Assert ArgumentError is thrown.
Assert no directory was created outside the projects root.
Record: { scenario: 'F', guard_fired: bool, path_escaped: bool,

pass: bool }

pass = guard_fired && !path_escaped


Write all scenario records to
`getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/folder_lifecycle_<deviceModel>.json`.

Assert after all scenarios:
```dart
final failures = results
    .where((r) => r['pass'] == false && r['pass'] != 'SKIPPED')
    .toList();
expect(failures, isEmpty,
    reason: 'Folder lifecycle failures: ${jsonEncode(failures)}');
```

- Missing folder manager class (CRITICAL)

Steps to reproduce:


<specific steps>


Proposed fix: <file path, method name, specific change>

Severity guide:
- Critical: captured frames not written (no folder created before capture)
- Critical: path traversal allows writes outside app sandbox
- High: orphaned folders not cleanable, storage leak confirmed

  30 seconds or leave the device unusable — skip and mark SKIPPED if

---

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `FolderManager` class does not exist → all unit tests marked FAILING
      with reason "FolderManager not yet implemented"; integration scenario A
      will produce `FILE_NOT_FOUND` from `FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()`;
      record this as CRITICAL in section 7 of the report and file
      BUG-QA-FOLDER-001
- [ ] `Directory.create(recursive: true)` throws on Android API 29+
      due to scoped storage for paths outside app-specific directories →
      document in section 6; the fix is to use only app-specific paths
      (`getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()`, not external storage)
- [ ] Scenario B forced-kill leaves a partially written JPEG (non-zero
      bytes but corrupt JPEG header) → `FrameQualityChannel.evaluate()`
      may throw `UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT`; record in the JSON as
      `{ "corrupt_frame_detected": true }`; this is a known risk, not
      a folder management failure per se — document in section 8
- [ ] Project ID collision (two test runs use the same ID) → scenario D
      may produce false failures; the harness must delete all `qa-*`
      prefixed project folders at the START of each run before scenario A
      begins; add a cleanup preamble to the harness
- [ ] `deleteProjectFolder()` called on a currently open file handle
      (Android does not lock files; iOS may) → on iOS, deletion of a file
      with an open handle may succeed silently or fail; scenario C tests
      this indirectly; document any iOS-specific behaviour in section 6
- [ ] `listProjectFolders()` does not exist on the folder manager →
      scenario B orphan detection falls back to direct `Directory.list()`
      call on the base projects directory; document the gap

---

- All integration test project IDs must use the `qa-` prefix
  (`qa-lifecycle-A`, `qa-d-001`, etc.) so they are distinguishable
  from real user projects and can be bulk-cleaned by prefix if needed
- The harness preamble must delete all existing `qa-*` prefixed folders
  before scenario A runs — stale folders from a previous test run must
  not affect results
- Unit tests must be runnable with `dart test` (no Flutter binding) —
  use `Directory.systemTemp` as the injected base path, not
  `path_provider`
- Scenario E (storage pressure) must never leave the device in a
  low-storage state after the test completes — the dummy fill file must
  be deleted in a `tearDown` block, not inline, so it is cleaned up
  even if the scenario throws
- `folder_audit.md` must document exact relative paths, not vague
  descriptions — "the capture folder" is not acceptable; the exact path
  such as `<documents>/projects/<uuid>/frames/` is required

  Generate a coding prompt for this task: Analytics: photo_captured, photo_rejected_blur, photo_rejected_motion, photo_warned_exposure — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Analytics / QA

  Here is the agent-ready coding prompt:

# NEW FEATURE: Analytics Events — photo_captured, photo_rejected_blur,
#               photo_rejected_motion, photo_warned_exposure
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: Analytics
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Implement the four analytics events that instrument the camera capture
decision pipeline. Every capture attempt must fire exactly one of
`photo_captured` or a rejection event, never both. Exposure warnings fire
independently of capture outcome. All four events share a common property
schema with event-specific extensions. This is a pure instrumentation task —
no UI changes, no capture logic changes, no sensor pipeline changes.

- [ ] Define the `CaptureAnalyticsEvent` sealed class hierarchy with four
      concrete subtypes, one per event
- [ ] Define the shared `CaptureEventProperties` value type carrying
      properties common to all four events
- [ ] Implement `CaptureAnalyticsService` — the single class responsible
      for building and dispatching events to the existing analytics sink
- [ ] Wire `CaptureAnalyticsService` into the capture decision call sites
      in the existing pipeline (the exact wiring points are specified below)
- [ ] Write unit tests covering event construction, property population,
      mutual exclusivity of capture/rejection events, and dispatch call
      counts

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/capture_controller.dart` — identify the exact
   lines where a capture succeeds (after `triggerCapture()` resolves),
   where a capture is suppressed due to blur (`isBlurry == true`), and
   where a capture is suppressed due to motion (stability score below
   threshold); these are the three wiring points for the capture events
2. `lib/features/camera/frame_quality/frame_quality_result.dart` —
   confirm `isAcceptable`, `blur.isBlurry`, `blur.score`,
   `exposure.isUnderexposed`, `exposure.isOverexposed`,
   `exposure.meanLuminance` field names and types; these populate
   event properties directly
3. `lib/features/camera/sensor/stability_score.dart` — confirm `score`,
   `confidence`, and `timestamp` fields; `score` and `confidence`
   populate rejection event properties
4. `lib/features/camera/sensor/pitch_band_evaluator.dart` and
   `lib/features/camera/sensor/pitch_level.dart` — confirm `PitchLevel`
   enum case names; the current active `PitchLevel` is a required property
   on all four events
5. Search `lib/` for the existing analytics sink — look for files named
   `analytics_service`, `analytics_client`, `event_tracker`, or similar;
   identify the method signature used to dispatch an event (e.g.
   `track(String eventName, Map<String, dynamic> properties)`); if no
   analytics sink exists, document as CRITICAL GAP and target a
   `_AnalyticsSink` interface defined in this task
6. `test/features/camera/` and `test/qa/` — read all existing test
   files for fake/mock patterns; do not duplicate established fakes
